# S5.2 P0 Paper Note — GPT-4V(ision) is a Generalist Web Agent, if Grounded

## Metadata

- **Title:** GPT-4V(ision) is a Generalist Web Agent, if Grounded
- **System name:** SeeAct
- **Authors:** Boyuan Zheng, Boyu Gou, Jihyung Kil, Huan Sun, Yu Su
- **Year:** 2024
- **Venue/status:** Proceedings of the 41st International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2024), PMLR Vol. 235, pp. 61349–61385
- **DOI:** Not listed by PMLR
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2401.01614
- **Final venue verified from:** PMLR ICML 2024 page
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation
- **Priority:** P0
- **BibTeX key:** `seeact2024`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper introduces **SeeAct**, a multimodal web agent that uses GPT-4V to visually understand webpages and generate actions for web tasks.

The key idea is:

```text
screenshot + task + previous actions
→ GPT-4V generates a textual action plan
→ grounding module maps the plan to an HTML element and operation
→ browser executes the action
```

The paper’s core message is very important for S5.2:

```text
GPT-4V is strong at understanding webpages and generating the right high-level action,
but it still struggles to ground that action to the exact webpage element.
```

So the title is precise:

```text
GPT-4V can be a generalist web agent, IF grounded.
```

For the thesis, this is a central grounding paper because it separates two abilities:

```text
Action generation = deciding what to do
Action grounding = selecting the correct element and executable operation
```

The main finding is that GPT-4V with **oracle grounding** reaches **51.1% online task success**, but automatic grounding remains much weaker. This proves that the bottleneck is not only reasoning or planning; it is the reliable mapping between visual understanding, DOM/HTML elements, and executable browser events.

---

## Notes

- **Core idea:**  
  Use a large multimodal model, especially GPT-4V, as a generalist web agent by separating action generation from action grounding.

- **Key finding:**  
  GPT-4V can generate strong web-action intentions and reaches 51.1% online task success with oracle grounding, but automatic grounding has a large gap from oracle grounding.

- **Main limitation connected to thesis:**  
  The system still cannot reliably map a correct textual plan to the exact webpage element and operation. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters directly because extraction workflows require precise element selection, field targeting, and action execution.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the main S5.2 P0 paper showing that web-agent performance depends on multimodal perception and grounding, not only on LLM reasoning.

- **Connects to:**  
  Mind2Web, WebArena, VisualWebArena, WebVoyager, WebAgent, AutoWebGLM, Set-of-Marks prompting, DOM grounding, multimodal GUI agents, and visual-DOM alignment.

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

Zheng et al. introduced **SeeAct**, a multimodal web agent that uses GPT-4V for visual webpage understanding and web action generation. The paper is important because it decomposes web-agent behavior into two separate capabilities: **action generation**, where the model decides what should be done next, and **action grounding**, where the textual action description is converted into an executable browser event by selecting the correct HTML element, operation, and value. On Mind2Web and live websites, GPT-4V demonstrates strong potential as a generalist web agent, especially when oracle grounding is provided. In online evaluation, SeeAct with oracle grounding reaches 51.1% task success, substantially outperforming text-only baselines. However, the paper also shows that grounding remains the central bottleneck: automatic grounding strategies, including textual choices, element attributes, and image annotation, remain far below oracle grounding. For this thesis, SeeAct provides direct evidence that generalized web automation and data extraction require robust DOM/visual grounding, not only stronger LLM reasoning.

---

## Why this paper matters for my thesis

Your thesis is about:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because any generalized web agent must solve this mapping:

```text
user instruction
→ current webpage observation
→ intended next action
→ exact DOM element / visual target
→ executable browser operation
```

SeeAct shows that LMMs like GPT-4V can often understand the page and decide what action should happen next. But the system still fails when it must ground that decision precisely.

This is crucial for web data extraction. A data extraction agent must not only understand that it needs to extract a price, address, date, profile, or table. It must also:

```text
find the correct region or element
distinguish similar elements
avoid wrong buttons/forms
handle hidden or duplicated DOM elements
align screenshot evidence with HTML nodes
verify that the selected field is correct
```

So SeeAct directly supports the S5.2 thesis claim:

```text
Grounding is the bridge between reasoning and reliable web automation.
```

---

## Important concepts to remember

### 1. Action generation

Action generation means producing a natural-language description of the next step.

Example:

```text
Click the “Find Your Truck” button below the rental details.
```

This is not yet executable.

### 2. Action grounding

Action grounding means converting the textual action into a browser-executable command:

```text
Element: <input id=19 button find your truck />
Operation: CLICK
Value: None
```

This is the central S5.2 concept.

### 3. Element grounding

Element grounding is the hardest part of action grounding. The agent must identify the correct webpage element from many possible candidates.

### 4. Oracle grounding

Oracle grounding means a human manually maps the model’s intended action to the correct element. It measures the upper bound of action generation quality.

### 5. Textual-choice grounding

The model receives candidate HTML elements as multiple-choice options and selects one. This is the strongest automatic grounding method in the paper.

### 6. Image-annotation grounding

The model receives a screenshot with bounding boxes and labels. The paper shows this can fail badly on dense webpage screenshots because GPT-4V may hallucinate labels or confuse nearby labels.

### 7. Visual-DOM alignment

The best grounding strategy must use both:

```text
visual screenshot information
+ HTML/DOM structure
```

This matters because webpages are not just images and not just HTML.

---

## Key evidence from the paper

### 1. HTML is too long and noisy

The paper gives a striking example: a webpage screenshot may contain hundreds of HTML elements and require very large token budgets if represented as raw HTML, while the visual representation is much more compact.

Thesis meaning:

```text
raw HTML alone is inefficient and incomplete
```

This motivates multimodal page representation.

### 2. GPT-4V has strong action-generation ability

With oracle grounding, GPT-4V achieves strong offline and online results. This means the model often knows what to do.

Thesis meaning:

```text
planning and understanding are improving faster than grounding
```

### 3. Grounding is the main bottleneck

The paper compares several grounding methods:

```text
element attributes
textual choices
image annotation
oracle grounding
```

Textual choices perform best among automatic methods, but oracle grounding remains much stronger.

Thesis meaning:

```text
the agent can plan correctly but fail because it clicks the wrong element
```

### 4. Image annotation / Set-of-Marks is not enough

The paper finds that image annotation can cause hallucination and label-linking errors on complex webpage screenshots.

Common failures include:

```text
making up bounding-box labels
linking the correct box to the wrong label
confusing nearby elements
```

Thesis meaning:

```text
visual markers help, but they do not solve dense webpage grounding
```

### 5. Online evaluation differs from offline evaluation

The paper shows that online task success can differ substantially from offline success because multiple valid action paths may complete the same task.

Thesis meaning:

```text
static reference trajectories underestimate or misrepresent real agent capability
```

This connects back to S5.1.

---

This connects directly to S5.1:

```text
Mind2Web = offline action prediction benchmark
SeeAct = shows offline evaluation is incomplete because online paths vary
```

The paper supports the S5.1 conclusion:

```text
trajectory matching is not enough;
web agents need live/functional evaluation.
```

## Connection to S5.2

This paper is the S5.2 anchor because it gives a clean decomposition of web-agent grounding:

```text
Observation:
  screenshot + HTML

Reasoning:
  action generation

Grounding:
  element selection + operation + value

Execution:
  browser event
```

This is the correct technical bridge between S4 systems and S5.2 mechanisms.

  Live websites create practical issues: popups, dynamic pages, safety monitoring, and real-world side effects.

## Limitation connected to thesis

SeeAct is powerful but not sufficient for generalized web automation and data extraction.

It improves:

```text
multimodal webpage understanding
+ high-level action generation
+ online evaluation
```

But it does not fully solve:

```text
reliable DOM grounding
+ visual-DOM alignment
+ duplicated element disambiguation
+ long-horizon grounding error accumulation
+ safe autonomous execution
+ structured extraction verification
+ cost-efficient multimodal processing
```

For the thesis, the most important limitation is:

```text
Correct reasoning is not enough if the agent cannot ground actions reliably.
```

This limitation connects directly to the thesis gap:

```text
generalized web automation requires robust grounding before it can become reliable data extraction.
```

---

## Failure modes to remember

### 1. Making up bounding-box labels

The model describes the correct element but incorrectly assumes that it has a bounding-box label.

### 2. Wrong label association

The model identifies the right visual region but chooses the wrong nearby label.

### 3. Identical elements

The page may contain multiple identical buttons, such as several “Schedule” buttons. The model may choose the first plausible one.

### 4. Grounding-error cascades

One wrong click changes the page state and causes later actions to fail.

### 5. Offline/online path mismatch

A model may follow a path different from the ground-truth trace but still solve the task online.

# MarkupLM: Pre-training of Text and Markup Language for Visually Rich Document Understanding

## Metadata

- **Short name:** MarkupLM
- **Authors:** Junlong Li, Yiheng Xu, Lei Cui, Furu Wei
- **Year:** 2022
- **Venue/status:** ACL 2022 Long Papers
- **DOI:** 10.18653/v1/2022.acl-long.420
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2110.08518
- **Venue/status source:** ACL Anthology
- **S5.2 cluster:** Markup/HTML representation pretraining
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `markuplm2022`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Pre-trains a Transformer over text plus markup structure, using XPath embeddings as structural signals for HTML/XML documents.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Pre-trains a Transformer over text plus markup structure, using XPath embeddings as structural signals for HTML/XML documents.

- **Key finding:**  
  MarkupLM improves HTML-based document understanding on WebSRC and SWDE, showing that markup hierarchy is useful when fixed 2D layout is unavailable or dynamic.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It is a representation model for document understanding, not a live web agent; for the thesis, it is background for DOM/markup-aware perception rather than a complete automation system.

- **Connects to:**  
  S5.2 DOM/markup representation; S6 structured web extraction; S2 representation learning.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to explain the pre-agent era of markup-aware webpage/document understanding.

---

## Detailed notes

- HTML/XML documents are dynamically rendered, so fixed-layout document models such as LayoutLM do not directly transfer.
- XPath embeddings act as a structural substitute for 2D layout embeddings.
- Pretraining objectives include masked markup language modeling, node relationship prediction, and title-page matching.
- Important for web data extraction because SWDE is a structured web extraction benchmark.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

MarkupLM contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Pre-trains a Transformer over text plus markup structure, using XPath embeddings as structural signals for HTML/XML documents. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, It is a representation model for document understanding, not a live web agent; for the thesis, it is background for DOM/markup-aware perception rather than a complete automation system. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

## One-sentence summary

MarkupLM shows that **Pre-trains a Transformer over text plus markup structure, using XPath embeddings as structural signals for HTML/XML documents**, but the thesis still needs robust grounding and extraction-oriented verification.

# Understanding HTML with Large Language Models

## Metadata

- **Short name:** LLM4HTML
- **Authors:** Izzeddin Gur, Ofir Nachum, Yingjie Miao, Mustafa Safdari, Austin Huang, Aakanksha Chowdhery, Sharan Narang, Noah Fiedel, Aleksandra Faust
- **Year:** 2023
- **Venue/status:** Findings of EMNLP 2023

- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2210.03945
- **Venue/status source:** ACL Anthology

- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `gur2023html`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Studies whether pretrained LLMs can understand raw HTML for semantic classification, description generation, and autonomous web navigation.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Studies whether pretrained LLMs can understand raw HTML for semantic classification, description generation, and autonomous web navigation.

- **Key finding:**  
  Fine-tuned LLMs transfer strongly to HTML understanding and are much more sample-efficient than specialized supervised models on MiniWoB-style navigation.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  The approach is still text/HTML-centric and does not solve visual grounding, layout perception, or dynamic live-web interaction; for the thesis, it motivates why raw HTML alone is useful but incomplete.

- **Connects to:**  
  S5.2 HTML understanding; S5.3 navigation policies; S6 extraction; S4 early web agents.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the bridge from web-page representation learning to LLM-based web automation.

---

## Detailed notes

- Defines three canonical tasks: semantic classification of HTML elements, description generation, and autonomous web navigation.
- Shows that encoder-decoder models such as T5 are strong for HTML understanding.
- Provides evidence that pretrained LLMs can replace some custom HTML architectures.
- Still faces long-context and noisy-HTML issues that later S5.2 work tries to solve through contextualization, retrieval, and vision.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

Pix2Struct contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Pretrains an image-to-text model by reconstructing simplified HTML from masked webpage screenshots. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, It parses screenshots but does not itself perform long-horizon web actions; for the thesis, it is foundational for visual webpage perception, not a complete agent. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

## One-sentence summary

Pix2Struct shows that **Pretrains an image-to-text model by reconstructing simplified HTML from masked webpage screenshots**, but the thesis still needs robust grounding and extraction-oriented verification.

---

### D:\PhD\myArticlesAndPapers\Literature-review4\markdowns\S5\S5.2\P1\Part_1\2023-11 - Set-of-Mark Prompting Unleashes Extraordinary Visual Grounding in GPT-4V.md

# Set-of-Mark Prompting Unleashes Extraordinary Visual Grounding in GPT-4V

## Metadata

- **Short name:** Set-of-Mark / SoM
- **Authors:** Jianwei Yang, Hao Zhang, Feng Li, Xueyan Zou, Chunyuan Li, Jianfeng Gao
- **Year:** 2023
- **Venue/status:** arXiv preprint
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2310.11441
- **Venue/status source:** arXiv / project page
- **S5.2 cluster:** Visual prompting for grounding
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `yang2023som`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Overlays speakable visual marks such as numbers, letters, masks, or boxes on image regions so GPT-4V can refer to regions explicitly.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Overlays speakable visual marks such as numbers, letters, masks, or boxes on image regions so GPT-4V can refer to regions explicitly.

- **Key finding:**  
  SoM substantially improves GPT-4V grounding on fine-grained vision tasks and becomes a widely adopted grounding technique for visual web/GUI agents.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  SoM was effective on many visual tasks, but SeeAct later shows dense webpage screenshots can still cause hallucinated labels and wrong label-element linking; for the thesis, SoM is useful but not sufficient for reliable web grounding.

- **Connects to:**  
  SeeAct, WebVoyager, OmniParser, Magma, UI-TARS, visual grounding, S5.5 failure modes.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the visual-prompting method that influenced multimodal web-agent grounding.

---

## Detailed notes

- Partitions images into meaningful regions using segmentation models such as SAM/SEEM.
- Adds human-readable marks that the LMM can output in text.
- Shifts grounding from numeric coordinate generation to region selection.
- In web-agent contexts, marks must be generated from DOM, accessibility trees, or screen parsing models.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

|---|---|

| **S5.2** | Main relevance: perception, representation, grounding, context selection |

| **S5.5** | Grounding, parsing, and context errors become failure modes |
| **S6** | Web data extraction needs grounded fields, tables, values, and evidence |
| **S8** | Cost, latency, live-web robustness, and deployment constraints |

---

Set-of-Mark / SoM contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Overlays speakable visual marks such as numbers, letters, masks, or boxes on image regions so GPT-4V can refer to regions explicitly. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, SoM was effective on many visual tasks, but SeeAct later shows dense webpage screenshots can still cause hallucinated labels and wrong label-element linking; for the thesis, SoM is useful but not sufficient for reliable web grounding. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

## One-sentence summary

Set-of-Mark / SoM shows that **Overlays speakable visual marks such as numbers, letters, masks, or boxes on image regions so GPT-4V can refer to regions explicitly**, but the thesis still needs robust grounding and extraction-oriented verification.

# From Pixels to UI Actions: Learning to Follow Instructions via Graphical User Interfaces

## Metadata

- **Short name:** Pix2Act
- **Authors:** Peter Shaw, Mandar Joshi, James Cohan, Jonathan Berant, Panupong Pasupat, Hexiang Hu, Urvashi Khandelwal, Kenton Lee, Kristina Toutanova
- **Year:** 2023
- **Venue/status:** NeurIPS 2023
- **DOI:** Not listed in NeurIPS page
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2306.00245
- **Venue/status source:** NeurIPS proceedings
- **S5.2 cluster:** Pixel-only GUI action model
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `pix2act2023`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds a GUI agent using only screenshots and generic mouse/keyboard actions, without DOM, HTML, or task-specific action spaces.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds a GUI agent using only screenshots and generic mouse/keyboard actions, without DOM, HTML, or task-specific action spaces.

- **Key finding:**  
  With Pix2Struct pretraining and tree-search policy improvement, Pix2Act can outperform human crowdworkers on MiniWoB++ in the proposed pixel-only setting.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Strong mainly on controlled GUI benchmarks and weaker on broader web tasks such as WebShop; for the thesis, it proves pixel-only action is possible but not yet enough for generalized web extraction.

- **Connects to:**  
  Pix2Struct, SeeClick, CogAgent, UGround, OS-Atlas, UI-TARS.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to explain the shift from DOM-based web agents to human-like pixel-based GUI agents.

---

## Detailed notes

- Observation is a screenshot augmented with instruction and cursor state.
- Action space is generic: click, drag, key press, scroll.
- Does not use DOM or structured interface metadata.
- Shows pretraining on screenshot parsing is highly valuable for downstream GUI action learning.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, Strong mainly on controlled GUI benchmarks and weaker on broader web tasks such as WebShop; for the thesis, it proves pixel-only action is possible but not yet enough for generalized web extraction. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

# CogAgent: A Visual Language Model for GUI Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** CogAgent
- **Authors:** Wenyi Hong, Weihan Wang, Qingsong Lv, Jiazheng Xu, Wenmeng Yu, Junhui Ji, Yan Wang, Zihan Wang, Yuxuan Zhang, Juanzi Li, Bin Xu, Yuxiao Dong, Ming Ding, Jie Tang
- **Year:** 2024
- **Venue/status:** CVPR 2024
- **DOI:** Not listed
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2312.08914
- **Venue/status source:** CVF Open Access
- **S5.2 cluster:** High-resolution GUI VLM
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `cogagent2024`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Introduces an 18B visual language model specialized for GUI understanding and navigation using high-resolution image encoders.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Introduces an 18B visual language model specialized for GUI understanding and navigation using high-resolution image encoders.

- **Key finding:**  
  CogAgent can use screenshots only and outperform some HTML-consuming LLM agents on Mind2Web and AITW while maintaining strong VQA/document capabilities.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Large model and high-resolution processing remain expensive; for the thesis, it strengthens visual GUI perception but does not fully solve reliable live-web extraction or safety.

- **Connects to:**  
  SeeClick, OS-Atlas, UI-TARS, ShowUI, Magma.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as evidence that GUI-specific VLM training improves screen perception and action prediction.

---

## Detailed notes

- Uses low-resolution and high-resolution visual branches to detect small UI elements and text.
- Targets both PC/web and Android GUI navigation.
- Shows the need for GUI-domain data, not only natural-image pretraining.
- Important for S5.2 because perception quality directly affects grounding.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

- **DOI:** 10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.505

- **Venue/status source:** ACL Anthology
- **S5.2 cluster:** GUI grounding pretraining
- **Priority:** P1

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds a screenshot-only visual GUI agent and argues that GUI grounding is the key capability behind downstream GUI-agent performance.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

## Four-note template

  Builds a screenshot-only visual GUI agent and argues that GUI grounding is the key capability behind downstream GUI-agent performance.

  Grounding pretraining improves ScreenSpot and downstream MiniWob, AITW, and Mind2Web performance; better grounding correlates with better agent task execution.

- Automates GUI grounding data construction from web and mobile data.
- Shows screenshots can replace structured text in some agent settings.
- Supports the thesis claim that grounding quality directly drives web-agent success.

## Metadata

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds an LMM-powered web agent that uses screenshots plus marked interactive elements to complete tasks on real-world websites end-to-end.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds an LMM-powered web agent that uses screenshots plus marked interactive elements to complete tasks on real-world websites end-to-end.

- **Key finding:**  
  WebVoyager reports 59.1% task success on a 643-task benchmark from 15 websites and uses GPT-4V as an automatic evaluator with high agreement with human judgment.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Relies on closed-source LMMs and marked interactive elements; for the thesis, it shows practical live-web automation but does not solve robust structured extraction or safety under arbitrary websites.

### D:\PhD\myArticlesAndPapers\Literature-review4\markdowns\S5\S5.2\P1\Part_1\2024-08-OmniParser for Pure Vision Based GUI Agent.md

# OmniParser for Pure Vision Based GUI Agent

## Metadata

- **Short name:** OmniParser
- **Authors:** Yadong Lu, Jianwei Yang, Yelong Shen, Ahmed Awadallah
- **Year:** 2024
- **Venue/status:** ICLR 2025 submission / arXiv preprint; no final accepted venue found in this check
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2408.00203
- **Venue/status source:** OpenReview ICLR 2025 submission and arXiv
- **S5.2 cluster:** Vision-based screen parsing
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `omniparser2024`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Parses UI screenshots into structured interactable regions and semantic labels, improving GPT-4V action grounding without relying on DOM at inference.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Parses UI screenshots into structured interactable regions and semantic labels, improving GPT-4V action grounding without relying on DOM at inference.

- **Key finding:**  
  A screen parser can turn pure screenshots into structured regions that make multimodal agents easier to ground on ScreenSpot, Mind2Web, and AITW.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It adds another parsing component and its quality bounds downstream agent reliability; for the thesis, it helps reduce DOM dependence but introduces parser errors.

- **Connects to:**  
  SoM, WebVoyager, SeeAct, UI-TARS perception tasks, S5.5 parser/grounding failures.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the screen-parsing approach between raw pixels and structured GUI grounding.

---

## Detailed notes

- Creates interactable icon detection data from popular webpages.
- Combines detection and captioning modules to parse UI elements.
- Allows SoM-like prompting in settings without DOM or accessibility tree.
- Directly addresses the SeeAct limitation: GPT-4V needs reliable regions to ground actions.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

## Cross-links

| Later section | Connection |
|---|---|
| **S5.1** | Benchmark/evaluation context for web and GUI agents |
| **S5.2** | Main relevance: perception, representation, grounding, context selection |
| **S5.3** | Better observations improve planning and next-action decisions |
| **S5.5** | Grounding, parsing, and context errors become failure modes |
| **S6** | Web data extraction needs grounded fields, tables, values, and evidence |
| **S8** | Cost, latency, live-web robustness, and deployment constraints |

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

OmniParser contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Parses UI screenshots into structured interactable regions and semantic labels, improving GPT-4V action grounding without relying on DOM at inference. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, It adds another parsing component and its quality bounds downstream agent reliability; for the thesis, it helps reduce DOM dependence but introduces parser errors. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

## One-sentence summary

OmniParser shows that **Parses UI screenshots into structured interactable regions and semantic labels, improving GPT-4V action grounding without relying on DOM at inference**, but the thesis still needs robust grounding and extraction-oriented verification.

# OS-ATLAS: A Foundation Action Model for Generalist GUI Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** OS-Atlas
- **Authors:** Zhiyong Wu, Zhenyu Wu, Fangzhi Xu, Yian Wang, Qiushi Sun, Chengyou Jia, Kanzhi Cheng, Zichen Ding, Liheng Chen, Paul Pu Liang, Yu Qiao
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** ICLR 2025 Spotlight
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2410.23218
- **Venue/status source:** ICLR 2025 / OpenReview
- **S5.2 cluster:** Foundation action model for GUI agents
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `osatlas2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds a generalist GUI action model using a large cross-platform GUI grounding corpus and unified action modeling across web, desktop, and mobile.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds a generalist GUI action model using a large cross-platform GUI grounding corpus and unified action modeling across web, desktop, and mobile.

- **Key finding:**  
  OS-Atlas improves OOD GUI grounding and agentic tasks over previous open-source models, showing that scale and cross-platform grounding data matter.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Large-scale data and training are required, and generalized action prediction still does not guarantee task-level safety or extraction verification; for the thesis, it is strong grounding infrastructure but not a web-extraction solution by itself.

- **Connects to:**  
  SeeClick, UGround, UI-TARS, ShowUI, Magma.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to discuss scaling laws/data infrastructure for generalist GUI grounding.

---

## Detailed notes

- Releases a synthesis toolkit for GUI grounding data across Windows, Linux, macOS, Android, and the web.
- Curates over 13M GUI elements from more than 2.3M screenshots.
- Works in grounding mode, action mode, and agent mode.
- Addresses action naming conflicts across platforms.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

Builds a generalist GUI action model using a large cross-platform GUI grounding corpus and unified action modeling across web, desktop, and mobile. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

# Navigating the Digital World as Humans Do: Universal Visual Grounding for GUI Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** UGround
- **Authors:** Boyu Gou, Ruohan Wang, Boyuan Zheng, Yanan Xie, Cheng Chang, Yiheng Shu, Huan Sun, Yu Su
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** ICLR 2025 Oral
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2410.05243
- **Venue/status source:** ICLR 2025 / official project and OpenReview
- **S5.2 cluster:** Universal visual grounding
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `uground2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Trains UGround, a universal visual grounding model that maps GUI referring expressions to pixel coordinates across web, desktop, and mobile.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Trains UGround, a universal visual grounding model that maps GUI referring expressions to pixel coordinates across web, desktop, and mobile.

- **Key finding:**  
  UGround uses 10M GUI elements over 1.3M screenshots and improves grounding, offline agent, and online agent performance while using only visual perception.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Visual-only grounding still needs a planner and does not by itself validate whether an extracted answer is correct; for the thesis, it solves target localization better but not full web data extraction.

- **Connects to:**  
  SeeAct-V, SeeClick, OS-Atlas, GUI-Actor, S5.5 grounding failures.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as a central P1 follow-up to SeeAct: specialized grounding models can reduce the oracle-grounding gap.

---

## Detailed notes

- Argues for human-like GUI embodiment: screenshots as observation and pixel-level actions.
- Decouples planning and grounding in SeeAct-V.
- Requires high accuracy, generalization, and flexibility from grounding models.
- Strongly supports thesis claim that grounding is the operational bottleneck of web agents.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

UGround contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Trains UGround, a universal visual grounding model that maps GUI referring expressions to pixel coordinates across web, desktop, and mobile. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, Visual-only grounding still needs a planner and does not by itself validate whether an extracted answer is correct; for the thesis, it solves target localization better but not full web data extraction. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

# ShowUI: One Vision-Language-Action Model for GUI Visual Agent

## Metadata

- **Short name:** ShowUI
- **Authors:** Kevin Qinghong Lin, Linjie Li, Difei Gao, Zhengyuan Yang, Shiwei Wu, Zechen Bai, Weixian Lei, Lijuan Wang, Mike Zheng Shou
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** CVPR 2025
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2411.17465
- **Venue/status source:** CVF Open Access / GitHub
- **S5.2 cluster:** Vision-language-action GUI model
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `showui2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds a lightweight vision-language-action model for GUI agents with UI-guided visual token selection and interleaved vision-language-action streaming.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds a lightweight vision-language-action model for GUI agents with UI-guided visual token selection and interleaved vision-language-action streaming.

- **Key finding:**  
  ShowUI reaches strong screenshot grounding with only 2B parameters and reduces redundant visual tokens by 33%, improving training efficiency.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Efficiency and grounding improve, but the system remains benchmark-focused and does not guarantee robust live-web extraction; for the thesis, it supports cost-efficient perception as a deployment concern.

- **Connects to:**  
  UI-TARS, Magma, OS-Atlas, S8 cost efficiency.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to discuss efficient visual-token processing and action modeling in GUI agents.

---

## Detailed notes

- Models UI screenshots as connected graphs to select informative visual tokens.
- Represents actions in JSON-like formats for unified action learning.
- Uses a smaller high-quality GUI instruction-following dataset rather than only scaling data.
- Important for the S8 deployment angle because visual-agent cost is a recurring bottleneck.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

### D:\PhD\myArticlesAndPapers\Literature-review4\markdowns\S5\S5.2\P1\Part_1\2025-02 - Magma- A Foundation Model for Multimodal AI Agents.md

# Magma: A Foundation Model for Multimodal AI Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** Magma
- **Authors:** Jianwei Yang, Reuben Tan, Qianhui Wu, Ruijie Zheng, Baolin Peng, Yongyuan Liang, Yu Gu, Mu Cai, Seonghyeon Ye, Joel Jang, Yuquan Deng, Lars Liden, Jianfeng Gao
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** CVPR 2025
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2502.13130
- **Venue/status source:** CVF Open Access
- **S5.2 cluster:** Multimodal foundation model for agentic action
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `magma2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Extends VLMs into multimodal agents by training on heterogeneous vision, video, UI, and robotics data using Set-of-Mark and Trace-of-Mark signals.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Extends VLMs into multimodal agents by training on heterogeneous vision, video, UI, and robotics data using Set-of-Mark and Trace-of-Mark signals.

- **Key finding:**  
  Magma shows that SoM and ToM can bridge verbal understanding and spatial-temporal action planning across digital and physical environments.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Very broad agentic scope may dilute web-specific extraction analysis; for the thesis, use it as a general foundation-model trend, not the main web-grounding evidence.

- **Connects to:**  
  SoM, UI-TARS, ShowUI, robotics VLA, S5.4 training.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to discuss the broader convergence of web/GUI agents with multimodal action foundation models.

---

## Detailed notes

- Uses SoM for actionable objects in images and ToM for movements in videos.
- Targets UI navigation, visual-language understanding, and robotic manipulation.
- Important because it treats action grounding and planning as pretraining objectives.
- Shows web/GUI automation is part of a wider multimodal agent modeling trend.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

## Cross-links

| Later section | Connection |
|---|---|
| **S5.1** | Benchmark/evaluation context for web and GUI agents |
| **S5.2** | Main relevance: perception, representation, grounding, context selection |
| **S5.3** | Better observations improve planning and next-action decisions |
| **S5.5** | Grounding, parsing, and context errors become failure modes |
| **S6** | Web data extraction needs grounded fields, tables, values, and evidence |
| **S8** | Cost, latency, live-web robustness, and deployment constraints |

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

Magma contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Extends VLMs into multimodal agents by training on heterogeneous vision, video, UI, and robotics data using Set-of-Mark and Trace-of-Mark signals. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, Very broad agentic scope may dilute web-specific extraction analysis; for the thesis, use it as a general foundation-model trend, not the main web-grounding evidence. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

## One-sentence summary

Magma shows that **Extends VLMs into multimodal agents by training on heterogeneous vision, video, UI, and robotics data using Set-of-Mark and Trace-of-Mark signals**, but the thesis still needs robust grounding and extraction-oriented verification.

---

# Learning to Contextualize Web Pages for Enhanced Decision Making by LLM Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** LCoW
- **Authors:** Dongjun Lee, Juyong Lee, Kyuyoung Kim, Jihoon Tack, Jinwoo Shin, Yee Whye Teh, Kimin Lee
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** ICLR 2025
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2503.10689
- **Venue/status source:** ICLR 2025 / OpenReview / arXiv
- **S5.2 cluster:** Web-page contextualization / observation simplification
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `lcow2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Decouples web-page understanding from decision-making by training a contextualization module that rewrites complex raw observations into focused, comprehensible observations for LLM agents.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Decouples web-page understanding from decision-making by training a contextualization module that rewrites complex raw observations into focused, comprehensible observations for LLM agents.

- **Key finding:**  
  LCoW improves success rates for closed-source and open-source agents on WorkArena and achieves strong WebShop performance.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It improves decision context but still depends on the quality of the contextualizer and underlying raw observation; for the thesis, it helps observation reduction but does not solve source-grounded extraction verification.

- **Connects to:**  
  LineRetriever, WebAgent HTML summarization, S5.2 observation representation, S6 extraction.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the main P1 paper for contextualizing raw HTML/accessibility-tree observations.

---

## Detailed notes

- Transforms raw, long web observations into focused observations with explanations.
- Trains the contextualizer using rewards based on whether action models predict correct actions.
- Shows raw observation complexity can be a larger bottleneck than reasoning ability.
- Very relevant to cost-efficient web automation and data extraction.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

Decouples web-page understanding from decision-making by training a contextualization module that rewrites complex raw observations into focused, comprehensible observations for LLM agents. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

```text
Presents Surfer-H, a web agent using screenshot-only policy/localizer/validator modules, paired with Holo1 open-weight VLMs specialized for web navigation and information extraction.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

  Reports strong WebVoyager performance with much better cost-efficiency and releases WebClick, a web UI localization benchmark.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Currently a preprint/technical report and evaluated mainly on web-navigation benchmarks; for the thesis, useful for deployment/cost and open-weight direction, not definitive peer-reviewed evidence yet.

# GUI-Actor: Coordinate-Free Visual Grounding for GUI Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** GUI-Actor
- **Authors:** Qianhui Wu, Kanzhi Cheng, Rui Yang, Chaoyun Zhang, Jianwei Yang, Huiqiang Jiang, Jian Mu, Baolin Peng, Bo Qiao, Reuben Tan, Si Qin, Lars Liden, Qingwei Lin, Huan Zhang, Tong Zhang, Jianbing Zhang, Dongmei Zhang, Jianfeng Gao
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** NeurIPS 2025 Poster
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2506.03143
- **Venue/status source:** OpenReview / NeurIPS 2025 / Microsoft Research
- **S5.2 cluster:** Coordinate-free GUI grounding
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `guiactor2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Replaces coordinate-string generation with an attention-based action head that grounds GUI actions by attending over visual patch tokens.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Replaces coordinate-string generation with an attention-based action head that grounds GUI actions by attending over visual patch tokens.

- **Key finding:**  
  Coordinate-free grounding improves generalization to unseen layouts and resolutions; a verifier can select the best candidate region.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Still focused on action grounding, not full task-level extraction or source verification; for the thesis, it is directly relevant to reducing click/field localization errors.

- **Connects to:**  
  UGround, OS-Atlas, UI-TARS, ShowUI, S5.5 grounding failure modes.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as a late P1 improvement over coordinate-generation grounding.

---

## Detailed notes

- Identifies three limitations of coordinate generation: weak spatial-semantic alignment, ambiguous supervision targets, and feature/action granularity mismatch.
- Introduces a dedicated <ACTOR> token and attention-based action head.
- Uses multi-patch supervision to allow multiple valid click regions inside an element.
- Adds a grounding verifier for candidate region selection.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

|---|---|

| **S5.3** | Better observations improve planning and next-action decisions |
| **S5.5** | Grounding, parsing, and context errors become failure modes |
| **S6** | Web data extraction needs grounded fields, tables, values, and evidence |
| **S8** | Cost, latency, live-web robustness, and deployment constraints |

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

GUI-Actor contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Replaces coordinate-string generation with an attention-based action head that grounds GUI actions by attending over visual patch tokens. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, Still focused on action grounding, not full task-level extraction or source verification; for the thesis, it is directly relevant to reducing click/field localization errors. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

# LineRetriever: Planning-Aware Observation Reduction for Web Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** LineRetriever
- **Authors:** Imene Kerboua, Sahar Omidi Shayegan, Megh Thakkar, Xing Han Lù, Massimo Caccia, Véronique Eglin, Alexandre Aussem, Jérémy Espinas, Alexandre Lacoste

- **BibTeX key:** `lineretriever2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Uses a smaller language model to retrieve observation lines from AxTree/DOM-like representations that are most relevant for future web-agent planning.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Uses a smaller language model to retrieve observation lines from AxTree/DOM-like representations that are most relevant for future web-agent planning.

- **Key finding:**  
  LineRetriever reduces observation length while maintaining task performance, addressing context limits and noise in web-agent observations.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It reduces text observations but does not address visual grounding or structured extraction verification; for the thesis, it is useful for scalable web-page context selection.

- **Connects to:**  
  LCoW, Mind2Web reranking, WorkArena, S8 cost efficiency.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the P1 observation-reduction counterpart to LCoW.

---

## Detailed notes

- Critiques embedding-based retrieval for lacking planning awareness.
- Uses task, current observation, and action history to select relevant lines.
- Targets AxTree/DOM observation compression for large LLM agents.
- Directly supports the thesis need for cost-efficient context management in long web workflows.

---

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

### D:\PhD\myArticlesAndPapers\Literature-review4\markdowns\S5\S5.2\P1\Part_1\S5_2_P1_part1_markdowns_deep_verified_fixed\2022-03 - MarkupLM- Pre-training of Text and Markup Language for Visually-rich Document Understanding.md

# MarkupLM: Pre-training of Text and Markup Language for Visually Rich Document Understanding

## Metadata

- **Short name:** MarkupLM
- **Authors:** Junlong Li, Yiheng Xu, Lei Cui, Furu Wei
- **Year:** 2022
- **Venue/status:** ACL 2022 Long Papers
- **DOI:** 10.18653/v1/2022.acl-long.420
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2110.08518
- **Venue/status source:** ACL Anthology
- **S5.2 cluster:** Markup/HTML representation pretraining
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `markuplm2022`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Pre-trains a Transformer over text plus markup structure, using XPath embeddings as structural signals for HTML/XML documents.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Pre-trains a Transformer over text plus markup structure, using XPath embeddings as structural signals for HTML/XML documents.

- **Key finding:**  
  MarkupLM improves HTML-based document understanding on WebSRC and SWDE, showing that markup hierarchy is useful when fixed 2D layout is unavailable or dynamic.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It is a representation model for document understanding, not a live web agent; for the thesis, it is background for DOM/markup-aware perception rather than a complete automation system.

- **Connects to:**  
  S5.2 DOM/markup representation; S6 structured web extraction; S2 representation learning.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to explain the pre-agent era of markup-aware webpage/document understanding.

---

## Detailed notes

- HTML/XML documents are dynamically rendered, so fixed-layout document models such as LayoutLM do not directly transfer.
- XPath embeddings act as a structural substitute for 2D layout embeddings.
- Pretraining objectives include masked markup language modeling, node relationship prediction, and title-page matching.
- Important for web data extraction because SWDE is a structured web extraction benchmark.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

## Cross-links

| Later section | Connection |
|---|---|
| **S5.1** | Benchmark/evaluation context for web and GUI agents |
| **S5.2** | Main relevance: perception, representation, grounding, context selection |
| **S5.3** | Better observations improve planning and next-action decisions |
| **S5.5** | Grounding, parsing, and context errors become failure modes |
| **S6** | Web data extraction needs grounded fields, tables, values, and evidence |
| **S8** | Cost, latency, live-web robustness, and deployment constraints |

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

MarkupLM contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Pre-trains a Transformer over text plus markup structure, using XPath embeddings as structural signals for HTML/XML documents. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, It is a representation model for document understanding, not a live web agent; for the thesis, it is background for DOM/markup-aware perception rather than a complete automation system. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

## One-sentence summary

MarkupLM shows that **Pre-trains a Transformer over text plus markup structure, using XPath embeddings as structural signals for HTML/XML documents**, but the thesis still needs robust grounding and extraction-oriented verification.

---

# Understanding HTML with Large Language Models

## Metadata

- **Short name:** LLM4HTML
- **Authors:** Izzeddin Gur, Ofir Nachum, Yingjie Miao, Mustafa Safdari, Austin Huang, Aakanksha Chowdhery, Sharan Narang, Noah Fiedel, Aleksandra Faust
- **Year:** 2023
- **Venue/status:** Findings of EMNLP 2023
- **DOI:** 10.18653/v1/2023.findings-emnlp.185
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2210.03945
- **Venue/status source:** ACL Anthology
- **S5.2 cluster:** HTML understanding and raw-HTML agents
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `gur2023html`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Studies whether pretrained LLMs can understand raw HTML for semantic classification, description generation, and autonomous web navigation.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Studies whether pretrained LLMs can understand raw HTML for semantic classification, description generation, and autonomous web navigation.

- **Key finding:**  
  Fine-tuned LLMs transfer strongly to HTML understanding and are much more sample-efficient than specialized supervised models on MiniWoB-style navigation.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  The approach is still text/HTML-centric and does not solve visual grounding, layout perception, or dynamic live-web interaction; for the thesis, it motivates why raw HTML alone is useful but incomplete.

- **Connects to:**  
  S5.2 HTML understanding; S5.3 navigation policies; S6 extraction; S4 early web agents.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the bridge from web-page representation learning to LLM-based web automation.

---

## Detailed notes

- Defines three canonical tasks: semantic classification of HTML elements, description generation, and autonomous web navigation.
- Shows that encoder-decoder models such as T5 are strong for HTML understanding.
- Provides evidence that pretrained LLMs can replace some custom HTML architectures.
- Still faces long-context and noisy-HTML issues that later S5.2 work tries to solve through contextualization, retrieval, and vision.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

Studies whether pretrained LLMs can understand raw HTML for semantic classification, description generation, and autonomous web navigation. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, The approach is still text/HTML-centric and does not solve visual grounding, layout perception, or dynamic live-web interaction; for the thesis, it motivates why raw HTML alone is useful but incomplete. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

# Pix2Struct: Screenshot Parsing as Pretraining for Visual Language Understanding

## Metadata

- **Short name:** Pix2Struct
- **Authors:** Kenton Lee, Mandar Joshi, Iulia Turc, Hexiang Hu, Fangyu Liu, Julian Eisenschlos, Urvashi Khandelwal, Peter Shaw, Ming-Wei Chang, Kristina Toutanova
- **Year:** 2023
- **Venue/status:** ICML 2023, PMLR Vol. 202
- **DOI:** Not listed by PMLR
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2210.03347
- **Venue/status source:** ICML/PMLR and arXiv
- **S5.2 cluster:** Screenshot-to-structure pretraining
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `pix2struct2023`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Pretrains an image-to-text model by reconstructing simplified HTML from masked webpage screenshots.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Pretrains an image-to-text model by reconstructing simplified HTML from masked webpage screenshots.

- **Key finding:**  
  A single pixel-based pretrained model transfers to document, UI, illustration, and visual-language tasks, proving that web screenshots are a powerful source of visual-language supervision.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It parses screenshots but does not itself perform long-horizon web actions; for the thesis, it is foundational for visual webpage perception, not a complete agent.

- **Connects to:**  
  S5.2 visual page representation; Pix2Act; SeeAct; visual-DOM alignment; S6 visual extraction.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the technical foundation for pixel-only GUI/web agents.

---

## Detailed notes

- Uses web screenshots and corresponding HTML as pretraining pairs.
- Introduces variable-resolution input handling to preserve aspect ratios.
- Subsumes OCR, visual language modeling, and layout understanding in one screenshot parsing objective.
- Directly motivates Pix2Act and later visual GUI agents.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---
