- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the bridge from web-page representation learning to LLM-based web automation.

---

## Detailed notes

- Defines three canonical tasks: semantic classification of HTML elements, description generation, and autonomous web navigation.
- Shows that encoder-decoder models such as T5 are strong for HTML understanding.
- Provides evidence that pretrained LLMs can replace some custom HTML architectures.
- Still faces long-context and noisy-HTML issues that later S5.2 work tries to solve through contextualization, retrieval, and vision.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

# Pix2Struct: Screenshot Parsing as Pretraining for Visual Language Understanding

## Metadata

- **Short name:** Pix2Struct
- **Authors:** Kenton Lee, Mandar Joshi, Iulia Turc, Hexiang Hu, Fangyu Liu, Julian Eisenschlos, Urvashi Khandelwal, Peter Shaw, Ming-Wei Chang, Kristina Toutanova
- **Year:** 2023

- **DOI:** Not listed by PMLR

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Pretrains an image-to-text model by reconstructing simplified HTML from masked webpage screenshots.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Pretrains an image-to-text model by reconstructing simplified HTML from masked webpage screenshots.

- **Key finding:**  
  A single pixel-based pretrained model transfers to document, UI, illustration, and visual-language tasks, proving that web screenshots are a powerful source of visual-language supervision.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It parses screenshots but does not itself perform long-horizon web actions; for the thesis, it is foundational for visual webpage perception, not a complete agent.

- **Connects to:**  
  S5.2 visual page representation; Pix2Act; SeeAct; visual-DOM alignment; S6 visual extraction.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the technical foundation for pixel-only GUI/web agents.

---

## Detailed notes

- Uses web screenshots and corresponding HTML as pretraining pairs.
- Introduces variable-resolution input handling to preserve aspect ratios.
- Subsumes OCR, visual language modeling, and layout understanding in one screenshot parsing objective.
- Directly motivates Pix2Act and later visual GUI agents.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

# Set-of-Mark Prompting Unleashes Extraordinary Visual Grounding in GPT-4V

## Metadata

- **Short name:** Set-of-Mark / SoM
- **Authors:** Jianwei Yang, Hao Zhang, Feng Li, Xueyan Zou, Chunyuan Li, Jianfeng Gao
- **Year:** 2023
- **Venue/status:** arXiv preprint
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2310.11441
- **Venue/status source:** arXiv / project page
- **S5.2 cluster:** Visual prompting for grounding
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `yang2023som`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Overlays speakable visual marks such as numbers, letters, masks, or boxes on image regions so GPT-4V can refer to regions explicitly.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Overlays speakable visual marks such as numbers, letters, masks, or boxes on image regions so GPT-4V can refer to regions explicitly.

- **Key finding:**  
  SoM substantially improves GPT-4V grounding on fine-grained vision tasks and becomes a widely adopted grounding technique for visual web/GUI agents.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  SoM was effective on many visual tasks, but SeeAct later shows dense webpage screenshots can still cause hallucinated labels and wrong label-element linking; for the thesis, SoM is useful but not sufficient for reliable web grounding.

- **Connects to:**  
  SeeAct, WebVoyager, OmniParser, Magma, UI-TARS, visual grounding, S5.5 failure modes.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the visual-prompting method that influenced multimodal web-agent grounding.

---

## Detailed notes

- Partitions images into meaningful regions using segmentation models such as SAM/SEEM.
- Adds human-readable marks that the LMM can output in text.
- Shifts grounding from numeric coordinate generation to region selection.
- In web-agent contexts, marks must be generated from DOM, accessibility trees, or screen parsing models.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

Overlays speakable visual marks such as numbers, letters, masks, or boxes on image regions so GPT-4V can refer to regions explicitly. ## Metadata

- **Authors:** Peter Shaw, Mandar Joshi, James Cohan, Jonathan Berant, Panupong Pasupat, Hexiang Hu, Urvashi Khandelwal, Kenton Lee, Kristina Toutanova

- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2306.00245
- **Venue/status source:** NeurIPS proceedings
- **S5.2 cluster:** Pixel-only GUI action model
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `pix2act2023`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds a GUI agent using only screenshots and generic mouse/keyboard actions, without DOM, HTML, or task-specific action spaces.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds a GUI agent using only screenshots and generic mouse/keyboard actions, without DOM, HTML, or task-specific action spaces.

- **Key finding:**  
  With Pix2Struct pretraining and tree-search policy improvement, Pix2Act can outperform human crowdworkers on MiniWoB++ in the proposed pixel-only setting.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Strong mainly on controlled GUI benchmarks and weaker on broader web tasks such as WebShop; for the thesis, it proves pixel-only action is possible but not yet enough for generalized web extraction.

- **Connects to:**  
  Pix2Struct, SeeClick, CogAgent, UGround, OS-Atlas, UI-TARS.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to explain the shift from DOM-based web agents to human-like pixel-based GUI agents.

---

## Detailed notes

- Observation is a screenshot augmented with instruction and cursor state.
- Action space is generic: click, drag, key press, scroll.
- Does not use DOM or structured interface metadata.
- Shows pretraining on screenshot parsing is highly valuable for downstream GUI action learning.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

| Later section | Connection |
|---|---|
| **S5.1** | Benchmark/evaluation context for web and GUI agents |
| **S5.2** | Main relevance: perception, representation, grounding, context selection |
| **S5.3** | Better observations improve planning and next-action decisions |
| **S5.5** | Grounding, parsing, and context errors become failure modes |
| **S6** | Web data extraction needs grounded fields, tables, values, and evidence |
| **S8** | Cost, latency, live-web robustness, and deployment constraints |

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

Pix2Act contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Builds a GUI agent using only screenshots and generic mouse/keyboard actions, without DOM, HTML, or task-specific action spaces. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, Strong mainly on controlled GUI benchmarks and weaker on broader web tasks such as WebShop; for the thesis, it proves pixel-only action is possible but not yet enough for generalized web extraction. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

## One-sentence summary

Pix2Act shows that **Builds a GUI agent using only screenshots and generic mouse/keyboard actions, without DOM, HTML, or task-specific action spaces**, but the thesis still needs robust grounding and extraction-oriented verification.

---

# CogAgent: A Visual Language Model for GUI Agents

- **Short name:** CogAgent
- **Authors:** Wenyi Hong, Weihan Wang, Qingsong Lv, Jiazheng Xu, Wenmeng Yu, Junhui Ji, Yan Wang, Zihan Wang, Yuxuan Zhang, Juanzi Li, Bin Xu, Yuxiao Dong, Ming Ding, Jie Tang
- **Year:** 2024
- **Venue/status:** CVPR 2024
- **DOI:** Not listed

- **Venue/status source:** CVF Open Access
- **S5.2 cluster:** High-resolution GUI VLM
- **Priority:** P1

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Introduces an 18B visual language model specialized for GUI understanding and navigation using high-resolution image encoders.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Introduces an 18B visual language model specialized for GUI understanding and navigation using high-resolution image encoders.

- **Key finding:**  
  CogAgent can use screenshots only and outperform some HTML-consuming LLM agents on Mind2Web and AITW while maintaining strong VQA/document capabilities.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Large model and high-resolution processing remain expensive; for the thesis, it strengthens visual GUI perception but does not fully solve reliable live-web extraction or safety.

- **Connects to:**  
  SeeClick, OS-Atlas, UI-TARS, ShowUI, Magma.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as evidence that GUI-specific VLM training improves screen perception and action prediction.

---

## Detailed notes

- Uses low-resolution and high-resolution visual branches to detect small UI elements and text.
- Targets both PC/web and Android GUI navigation.
- Shows the need for GUI-domain data, not only natural-image pretraining.
- Important for S5.2 because perception quality directly affects grounding.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

Introduces an 18B visual language model specialized for GUI understanding and navigation using high-resolution image encoders. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, Large model and high-resolution processing remain expensive; for the thesis, it strengthens visual GUI perception but does not fully solve reliable live-web extraction or safety. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

# SeeClick: Harnessing GUI Grounding for Advanced Visual GUI Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** SeeClick
- **Authors:** Kanzhi Cheng, Qiushi Sun, Yougang Chu, Fangzhi Xu, Yantao Li, Jianbing Zhang, Zhiyong Wu
- **Year:** 2024
- **Venue/status:** ACL 2024 Long Papers
- **DOI:** 10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.505
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2401.10935
- **Venue/status source:** ACL Anthology
- **S5.2 cluster:** GUI grounding pretraining
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `seeclick2024`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds a screenshot-only visual GUI agent and argues that GUI grounding is the key capability behind downstream GUI-agent performance.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds a screenshot-only visual GUI agent and argues that GUI grounding is the key capability behind downstream GUI-agent performance.

- **Key finding:**  
  Grounding pretraining improves ScreenSpot and downstream MiniWob, AITW, and Mind2Web performance; better grounding correlates with better agent task execution.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Vision-only coordinate prediction still struggles with complex dynamic pages and fine-grained web semantics; for the thesis, grounding improves automation but extraction still needs verification and schema awareness.

- **Connects to:**  
  ScreenSpot, UGround, OS-Atlas, GUI-Actor, SeeAct.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as a major S5.2 P1 paper showing that grounding is not a small module but a core capability.

---

## Detailed notes

- Creates ScreenSpot, a realistic GUI grounding benchmark across mobile, desktop, and web.
- Automates GUI grounding data construction from web and mobile data.
- Shows screenshots can replace structured text in some agent settings.
- Supports the thesis claim that grounding quality directly drives web-agent success.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

Builds a screenshot-only visual GUI agent and argues that GUI grounding is the key capability behind downstream GUI-agent performance. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. ## Metadata

- **Short name:** WebVoyager
- **Authors:** Hongliang He, Wenlin Yao, Kaixin Ma, Wenhao Yu, Yong Dai, Hongming Zhang, Zhenzhong Lan, Dong Yu
- **Year:** 2024
- **Venue/status:** ACL 2024 Long Papers
- **DOI:** 10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.371
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2401.13919
- **Venue/status source:** ACL Anthology
- **S5.2 cluster:** End-to-end multimodal live-web agent
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `he2024webvoyager`

## Citation merge note

This is the same paper already used in S4 P0. Do **not** create a second BibTeX entry for S5.2.

Use one citation key everywhere:

```text
he2024webvoyager
```

This S5.2 note can still discuss the perception/grounding role of WebVoyager, but the bibliography entry must be shared with S4.

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds an LMM-powered web agent that uses screenshots plus marked interactive elements to complete tasks on real-world websites end-to-end.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds an LMM-powered web agent that uses screenshots plus marked interactive elements to complete tasks on real-world websites end-to-end.

- **Key finding:**  
  WebVoyager reports 59.1% task success on a 643-task benchmark from 15 websites and uses GPT-4V as an automatic evaluator with high agreement with human judgment.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Relies on closed-source LMMs and marked interactive elements; for the thesis, it shows practical live-web automation but does not solve robust structured extraction or safety under arbitrary websites.

- **Connects to:**  
  S5.1 open-web evaluation, SeeAct, SoM, S5.3 planning, S6 data extraction.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as a system-level example showing S5.2 perception/grounding deployed inside a live web agent.

---

## Detailed notes

- Observation combines screenshot image and textual web-element information.
- Actions include clicking, typing, scrolling, and answering.
- Uses Set-of-Mark-style element annotation to support action selection.
- Important for bridging S5.2 grounding and S5.3 end-to-end decision-making.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

## Venue verification note

Current citation status for this paper should remain:

```text
arXiv preprint; ICLR 2025 submission on OpenReview; no accepted final venue confirmed.
```

Microsoft released OmniParser/OmniParser tooling publicly, and there is also a separate **OmniParser V2** arXiv paper on visually-situated text parsing. These do **not** confirm a final accepted venue for **OmniParser for Pure Vision Based GUI Agent**. Therefore, cite this S5.2 paper as arXiv until a final venue is confirmed.

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Parses UI screenshots into structured interactable regions and semantic labels, improving GPT-4V action grounding without relying on DOM at inference.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Parses UI screenshots into structured interactable regions and semantic labels, improving GPT-4V action grounding without relying on DOM at inference.

- **Key finding:**  
  A screen parser can turn pure screenshots into structured regions that make multimodal agents easier to ground on ScreenSpot, Mind2Web, and AITW.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It adds another parsing component and its quality bounds downstream agent reliability; for the thesis, it helps reduce DOM dependence but introduces parser errors.

- **Connects to:**  
  SoM, WebVoyager, SeeAct, UI-TARS perception tasks, S5.5 parser/grounding failures.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the screen-parsing approach between raw pixels and structured GUI grounding.

---

## Detailed notes

- Creates interactable icon detection data from popular webpages.
- Combines detection and captioning modules to parse UI elements.
- Allows SoM-like prompting in settings without DOM or accessibility tree.
- Directly addresses the SeeAct limitation: GPT-4V needs reliable regions to ground actions.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

- **BibTeX key:** `osatlas2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds a generalist GUI action model using a large cross-platform GUI grounding corpus and unified action modeling across web, desktop, and mobile.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds a generalist GUI action model using a large cross-platform GUI grounding corpus and unified action modeling across web, desktop, and mobile.

- **Key finding:**  
  OS-Atlas improves OOD GUI grounding and agentic tasks over previous open-source models, showing that scale and cross-platform grounding data matter.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Large-scale data and training are required, and generalized action prediction still does not guarantee task-level safety or extraction verification; for the thesis, it is strong grounding infrastructure but not a web-extraction solution by itself.

- **Connects to:**  
  SeeClick, UGround, UI-TARS, ShowUI, Magma.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to discuss scaling laws/data infrastructure for generalist GUI grounding.

---

## Detailed notes

- Releases a synthesis toolkit for GUI grounding data across Windows, Linux, macOS, Android, and the web.
- Curates over 13M GUI elements from more than 2.3M screenshots.
- Works in grounding mode, action mode, and agent mode.
- Addresses action naming conflicts across platforms.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

OS-Atlas contributes to the S5.2 discussion by showing that web/GUI-agent reliability depends on how the interface is represented and grounded before action execution. Builds a generalist GUI action model using a large cross-platform GUI grounding corpus and unified action modeling across web, desktop, and mobile. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, Large-scale data and training are required, and generalized action prediction still does not guarantee task-level safety or extraction verification; for the thesis, it is strong grounding infrastructure but not a web-extraction solution by itself. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

OS-Atlas shows that **Builds a generalist GUI action model using a large cross-platform GUI grounding corpus and unified action modeling across web, desktop, and mobile**, but the thesis still needs robust grounding and extraction-oriented verification.

- **Year:** 2025

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Trains UGround, a universal visual grounding model that maps GUI referring expressions to pixel coordinates across web, desktop, and mobile.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Trains UGround, a universal visual grounding model that maps GUI referring expressions to pixel coordinates across web, desktop, and mobile.

- **Key finding:**  
  UGround uses 10M GUI elements over 1.3M screenshots and improves grounding, offline agent, and online agent performance while using only visual perception.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Visual-only grounding still needs a planner and does not by itself validate whether an extracted answer is correct; for the thesis, it solves target localization better but not full web data extraction.

- **Connects to:**  
  SeeAct-V, SeeClick, OS-Atlas, GUI-Actor, S5.5 grounding failures.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as a central P1 follow-up to SeeAct: specialized grounding models can reduce the oracle-grounding gap.

---

## Detailed notes

- Argues for human-like GUI embodiment: screenshots as observation and pixel-level actions.
- Decouples planning and grounding in SeeAct-V.
- Requires high accuracy, generalization, and flexibility from grounding models.
- Strongly supports thesis claim that grounding is the operational bottleneck of web agents.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

# ShowUI: One Vision-Language-Action Model for GUI Visual Agent

## Metadata

- **Short name:** ShowUI
- **Authors:** Kevin Qinghong Lin, Linjie Li, Difei Gao, Zhengyuan Yang, Shiwei Wu, Zechen Bai, Weixian Lei, Lijuan Wang, Mike Zheng Shou
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** CVPR 2025

- **Venue/status source:** CVF Open Access / GitHub

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Builds a lightweight vision-language-action model for GUI agents with UI-guided visual token selection and interleaved vision-language-action streaming.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Builds a lightweight vision-language-action model for GUI agents with UI-guided visual token selection and interleaved vision-language-action streaming.

- **Key finding:**  
  ShowUI reaches strong screenshot grounding with only 2B parameters and reduces redundant visual tokens by 33%, improving training efficiency.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Efficiency and grounding improve, but the system remains benchmark-focused and does not guarantee robust live-web extraction; for the thesis, it supports cost-efficient perception as a deployment concern.

- **Connects to:**  
  UI-TARS, Magma, OS-Atlas, S8 cost efficiency.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use to discuss efficient visual-token processing and action modeling in GUI agents.

---

## Detailed notes

- Models UI screenshots as connected graphs to select informative visual tokens.
- Represents actions in JSON-like formats for unified action learning.
- Uses a smaller high-quality GUI instruction-following dataset rather than only scaling data.
- Important for the S8 deployment angle because visual-agent cost is a recurring bottleneck.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

Builds a lightweight vision-language-action model for GUI agents with UI-guided visual token selection and interleaved vision-language-action streaming. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. ```

- **Venue/status:** ICLR 2025
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2503.10689
- **Venue/status source:** ICLR 2025 / OpenReview / arXiv
- **S5.2 cluster:** Web-page contextualization / observation simplification
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `lcow2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Decouples web-page understanding from decision-making by training a contextualization module that rewrites complex raw observations into focused, comprehensible observations for LLM agents.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Decouples web-page understanding from decision-making by training a contextualization module that rewrites complex raw observations into focused, comprehensible observations for LLM agents.

- **Key finding:**  
  LCoW improves success rates for closed-source and open-source agents on WorkArena and achieves strong WebShop performance.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It improves decision context but still depends on the quality of the contextualizer and underlying raw observation; for the thesis, it helps observation reduction but does not solve source-grounded extraction verification.

- **Connects to:**  
  LineRetriever, WebAgent HTML summarization, S5.2 observation representation, S6 extraction.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the main P1 paper for contextualizing raw HTML/accessibility-tree observations.

---

## Detailed notes

- Transforms raw, long web observations into focused observations with explanations.
- Trains the contextualizer using rewards based on whether action models predict correct actions.
- Shows raw observation complexity can be a larger bottleneck than reasoning ability.
- Very relevant to cost-efficient web automation and data extraction.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

Decouples web-page understanding from decision-making by training a contextualization module that rewrites complex raw observations into focused, comprehensible observations for LLM agents. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, It improves decision context but still depends on the quality of the contextualizer and underlying raw observation; for the thesis, it helps observation reduction but does not solve source-grounded extraction verification. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

# GUI-Actor: Coordinate-Free Visual Grounding for GUI Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** GUI-Actor
- **Authors:** Qianhui Wu, Kanzhi Cheng, Rui Yang, Chaoyun Zhang, Jianwei Yang, Huiqiang Jiang, Jian Mu, Baolin Peng, Bo Qiao, Reuben Tan, Si Qin, Lars Liden, Qingwei Lin, Huan Zhang, Tong Zhang, Jianbing Zhang, Dongmei Zhang, Jianfeng Gao
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** NeurIPS 2025 Poster
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2506.03143
- **Venue/status source:** OpenReview / NeurIPS 2025 / Microsoft Research
- **S5.2 cluster:** Coordinate-free GUI grounding
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `guiactor2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Replaces coordinate-string generation with an attention-based action head that grounds GUI actions by attending over visual patch tokens.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Replaces coordinate-string generation with an attention-based action head that grounds GUI actions by attending over visual patch tokens.

- **Key finding:**  
  Coordinate-free grounding improves generalization to unseen layouts and resolutions; a verifier can select the best candidate region.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  Still focused on action grounding, not full task-level extraction or source verification; for the thesis, it is directly relevant to reducing click/field localization errors.

- **Connects to:**  
  UGround, OS-Atlas, UI-TARS, ShowUI, S5.5 grounding failure modes.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as a late P1 improvement over coordinate-generation grounding.

---

## Detailed notes

- Identifies three limitations of coordinate generation: weak spatial-semantic alignment, ambiguous supervision targets, and feature/action granularity mismatch.
- Introduces a dedicated <ACTOR> token and attention-based action head.
- Uses multi-patch supervision to allow multiple valid click regions inside an element.
- Adds a grounding verifier for candidate region selection.

---

## Thesis relevance

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

The direct thesis connection is:

```text
better page representation / grounding
→ better action selection
→ more reliable web automation
→ more trustworthy data extraction
```

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is not simply model accuracy. The thesis-relevant gap is that current systems still do not jointly solve:

```text
robust page perception
+ reliable element grounding
+ long-context observation reduction
+ live-web changes
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ cost efficiency
```

So this paper should be used as part of the S5.2 technical decomposition, not as a final solution.

---

Replaces coordinate-string generation with an attention-based action head that grounds GUI actions by attending over visual patch tokens. The main lesson for the thesis is that perception and grounding are not auxiliary modules; they directly determine whether an LLM-based agent can select the correct element, preserve the relevant page state, and execute a valid action. However, Still focused on action grounding, not full task-level extraction or source verification; for the thesis, it is directly relevant to reducing click/field localization errors. This makes the paper useful for motivating the thesis gap around generalized, robust, and verifiable web automation and data extraction.

# LineRetriever: Planning-Aware Observation Reduction for Web Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** LineRetriever
- **Authors:** Imene Kerboua, Sahar Omidi Shayegan, Megh Thakkar, Xing Han Lù, Massimo Caccia, Véronique Eglin, Alexandre Aussem, Jérémy Espinas, Alexandre Lacoste

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Uses a smaller language model to retrieve observation lines from AxTree/DOM-like representations that are most relevant for future web-agent planning.
```

For the thesis, the important point is that this paper helps explain how web/GUI agents represent the current interface before making a decision. It is not only about planning. It is about how the agent sees the page, selects useful information, grounds actions, and keeps the observation manageable.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Uses a smaller language model to retrieve observation lines from AxTree/DOM-like representations that are most relevant for future web-agent planning.

- **Key finding:**  
  LineRetriever reduces observation length while maintaining task performance, addressing context limits and noise in web-agent observations.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It reduces text observations but does not address visual grounding or structured extraction verification; for the thesis, it is useful for scalable web-page context selection.

- **Connects to:**  
  LCoW, Mind2Web reranking, WorkArena, S8 cost efficiency.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the P1 observation-reduction counterpart to LCoW.

---

## Detailed notes

- Critiques embedding-based retrieval for lacking planning awareness.
- Uses task, current observation, and action history to select relevant lines.
- Targets AxTree/DOM observation compression for large LLM agents.
- Directly supports the thesis need for cost-efficient context management in long web workflows.

---

This paper supports the S5.2 claim that web agents require a reliable interface representation before they can act. For generalized web automation and data extraction, this matters because an agent must identify the right elements, ignore irrelevant page noise, preserve source evidence, and avoid grounding mistakes that cascade through a workflow.

```

### D:\PhD\myArticlesAndPapers\Literature-review4\markdowns\S5\S5.2\P1\S5_2_P1_complete_combined_fixed\Part_1\S5_2_P1_part1_mini_synthesis.md

# S5.2 P1 Part 1 Mini-Synthesis — Perception, Grounding, and Observation Reduction

## 1. What this batch adds after SeeAct

The S5.2 P0 paper, **SeeAct**, established the core bottleneck:

```text
LMMs can often decide what to do,
but they struggle to ground the action to the exact webpage element.
```

The S5.2 P1 Part 1 papers explain how the field tries to solve that bottleneck.

They expand the problem in five directions:

```text
1. HTML/markup representation
2. Screenshot parsing and visual webpage understanding
3. GUI grounding and coordinate prediction
4. Screen parsing and visual action models
5. Contextualization and observation reduction
```

Together, they show that S5.2 is not just about using screenshots. It is about building the right observation pipeline for agents.

---

## 2. Main technical evolution

### 2.1 From markup-aware models to HTML-aware LLMs

**MarkupLM** and **Understanding HTML with Large Language Models** show that HTML and markup structures contain important semantic signals.

The evolution is:

```text
markup hierarchy / XPath
→ raw HTML understanding
→ semantic classification and element description
→ action prediction over HTML pages
```

This is important for web data extraction because HTML structure can identify fields, tables, labels, forms, and repeated records.

But HTML-only methods remain limited:

```text
HTML is long
HTML is noisy
HTML may be incomplete
HTML may not match the rendered visual page
```

So the field moves toward visual page understanding.

---

### 2.2 From screenshots to structured visual understanding

**Pix2Struct** and **Pix2Act** show that screenshots can be treated as a primary representation.

Pix2Struct learns to parse screenshots into simplified HTML-like structures. Pix2Act then uses this visual pretraining to act in GUIs using only pixels and generic mouse/keyboard actions.

This shift matters because it changes the agent interface:

```text
DOM/action abstractions
→ pixel observations and human-like actions
```

For the thesis, this supports the idea that generalized web automation should not depend only on internal website code. A deployable agent may need to act visually, especially when DOM, APIs, or accessibility trees are unavailable or unreliable.

---

### 2.3 From visual prompting to GUI grounding

**Set-of-Mark**, **SeeClick**, **UGround**, **OS-Atlas**, and **GUI-Actor** all focus on grounding.

The key S5.2 idea is:

```text
the agent's plan must be mapped to a precise target region
```

Different methods solve this differently:

```text
SoM        → overlay labels on visual regions
SeeClick   → train GUI grounding directly
UGround    → train a universal cross-platform grounding model
OS-Atlas   → scale action/grounding data across platforms
GUI-Actor  → avoid coordinate-string generation via attention-based action grounding
```

This cluster confirms the SeeAct result: grounding is not a minor implementation detail. It is a central capability.

---

### 2.4 From modular perception to native visual agents

**CogAgent**, **ShowUI**, **UI-TARS**, and **Magma** move from grounding modules toward native visual/action models.

The trend is:

```text
separate perception + planner + grounding module
→ integrated vision-language-action model
```

These papers add new ingredients:

```text
high-resolution GUI perception
UI-guided visual token selection
unified action spaces
System-2 reasoning traces
reflective online training
multimodal action pretraining
```

For the thesis, these papers are important because they show the future direction of web agents: fewer hand-built wrappers and more data-driven agent models.

But the thesis must be careful: stronger native agents still do not automatically solve structured extraction, source verification, safety, or cost.

---

### 2.5 From raw observations to focused context

**LCoW** and **LineRetriever** address another S5.2 bottleneck: observations are too long and noisy.

Their shared idea is:

```text
do not feed the full page;
feed the parts that matter for the next decision.
```

LCoW contextualizes raw observations into a focused, explanatory representation. LineRetriever retrieves observation lines that are useful for future planning.

This is important for the thesis because generalized web extraction often involves long pages, large DOM trees, repeated elements, and multi-step workflows. Observation reduction is not only a cost trick; it is a reliability mechanism.

---

## 3. Central S5.2 P1 Part 1 gap

This batch shows strong progress, but the central gap remains:

```text
No current method jointly provides:
accurate GUI/web grounding
+ visual-DOM alignment
+ planning-aware context reduction
+ live-web robustness
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ low cost and latency
```

Each group solves one part:

```text
MarkupLM / HTML LLMs → structure
Pix2Struct / Pix2Act → visual representation and pixel actions
SoM / SeeClick / UGround / OS-Atlas / GUI-Actor → grounding
OmniParser → screen parsing
WebVoyager / Surfer-H → live multimodal web agents
LCoW / LineRetriever → observation reduction
UI-TARS / Magma / ShowUI → native visual-action agents
```

But no group fully solves generalized web automation and data extraction.

---

## 4. Thesis-ready synthesis paragraph

The S5.2 P1 literature extends SeeAct's grounding bottleneck into a broader architecture problem. Early representation work such as MarkupLM and HTML-oriented LLMs shows that markup structure and raw HTML contain useful semantic signals for web understanding, but HTML remains noisy, long, and incomplete. Pix2Struct and Pix2Act shift the field toward screenshot-based perception and pixel-level actions, showing that agents can learn from rendered interfaces rather than relying only on DOM trees. Set-of-Mark prompting, SeeClick, UGround, OS-Atlas, and GUI-Actor then identify visual grounding as the central operational capability: the agent must map a textual plan or instruction to the correct screen region. OmniParser adds a complementary screen-parsing layer that converts screenshots into structured regions. WebVoyager and Surfer-H show how these ideas can be deployed in live web agents, while LCoW and LineRetriever address the cost and noise of long web observations through contextualization and planning-aware reduction. Finally, ShowUI, UI-TARS, and Magma indicate a broader shift toward native vision-language-action models that integrate perception, grounding, reasoning, and action. Overall, S5.2 P1 shows that reliable web automation depends on the quality of the agent's observation pipeline, not only on the reasoning ability of the underlying LLM. The remaining thesis gap is to connect robust grounding and observation reduction with structured, source-verifiable web data extraction under live-web constraints.

---

## 5. Cross-links to later sections

| Cluster | Papers | Feeds |
|---|---|---|
| Markup/HTML representation | MarkupLM, Understanding HTML with LLMs | S6 extraction; DOM/schema understanding |
| Screenshot parsing | Pix2Struct, Pix2Act | S5.2 perception; S5.5 visual failures |
| Visual prompting/screen parsing | SoM, OmniParser | S5.2 grounding; S5.5 hallucinated labels |
| GUI grounding | SeeClick, UGround, OS-Atlas, GUI-Actor | S5.2 grounding; S8 deployment |
| End-to-end visual web agents | WebVoyager, Surfer-H | S5.3 planning; S8 cost/live deployment |
| Native action models | CogAgent, ShowUI, UI-TARS, Magma | S5.4 training; S5.3 reasoning |
| Context reduction | LCoW, LineRetriever | S8 cost; S6 extraction context selection |


---

For **OmniParser**, cite the GUI-agent paper as arXiv/preprint until a final accepted venue is confirmed.

## S5.2 Part 1 narrative

The first S5.2 P1 batch expands SeeAct's grounding bottleneck into a broader technical landscape:

```text
1. Markup/HTML representation: MarkupLM, Understanding HTML with LLMs
2. Screenshot-to-structure pretraining: Pix2Struct, Pix2Act
3. Visual prompting and screen parsing: SoM, OmniParser
4. Screenshot-only grounding/action models: CogAgent, SeeClick, UGround, OS-Atlas, ShowUI, UI-TARS, GUI-Actor
5. End-to-end multimodal web agents: WebVoyager, Surfer-H/Holo1
6. Context reduction/contextualization: LCoW, LineRetriever
7. General multimodal agent foundation models: Magma
```

## Main S5.2 conclusion from Part 1

```text
The field moves from HTML/DOM understanding
→ visual screenshot parsing
→ GUI grounding pretraining
→ universal action models
→ contextualized and cost-efficient web observations.
```

The thesis gap remains:

```text
No method jointly solves robust visual-DOM grounding,
planning-aware observation reduction, live-web action reliability,
structured extraction correctness, source verification, and cost efficiency.
```

### D:\PhD\myArticlesAndPapers\Literature-review4\markdowns\S5\S5.2\P1\S5_2_P1_complete_combined_fixed\Part_2\2025-10 - FocusAgent- Simple Yet Effective Ways of Trimming the Large Context of Web Agents.md

# FOCUSAGENT: Simple Yet Effective Ways of Trimming the Large Context of Web Agents

## Metadata

- **Short name:** FocusAgent
- **Authors:** Imene Kerboua, Sahar Omidi Shayegan, Megh Thakkar, Xing Han Lù, Léo Boisvert, Massimo Caccia, Jérémy Espinas, Alexandre Aussem, Véronique Eglin, Alexandre Lacoste
- **Year:** 2025
- **Venue/status:** arXiv preprint; OpenReview ICLR 2026 submission, no final accepted venue found in this check
- **DOI:** Not found
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2510.03204
- **Venue/status source:** arXiv + OpenReview ICLR 2026 submission page
- **S5.2 cluster:** Planning-aware AxTree observation pruning and security
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `focusagent2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Uses a lightweight LLM retriever to extract task-relevant lines from accessibility-tree observations before the main web agent predicts the next action.
```

For the thesis, this paper is important because S5.2 is about what the agent actually receives as its page state before deciding. A web agent does not fail only because it cannot reason; it often fails because the page observation is too long, too noisy, too visual, too dynamic, or not grounded to the correct element.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Uses a lightweight LLM retriever to extract task-relevant lines from accessibility-tree observations before the main web agent predicts the next action.

- **Key finding:**  
  FocusAgent reduces observation size by over 50% while maintaining performance close to full-observation baselines, and its defense variant strongly reduces banner/pop-up prompt-injection success rates.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  The retriever is still prompt-sensitive, and aggressive pruning can remove necessary state information; popup attacks can still block task execution even when malicious text is removed.

- **Connects to:**  
  LineRetriever, LCoW, WorkArena, WebArena, DoomArena, S8 cost/safety, S5.5 prompt-injection failures.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the S5.2 P1 continuation of LineRetriever: observation reduction becomes both a cost mechanism and a security mechanism.

---

## Detailed notes

- Problem: AxTree observations are shorter than DOMs but can still exceed tens of thousands of tokens.
- Pipeline: Stage 1 retrieves relevant AxTree line ranges; Stage 2 predicts actions from the pruned observation.
- The retriever receives the task goal, numbered observation lines, and optionally interaction history.
- The paper finds soft retrieval better than aggressive/neutral retrieval because uncertainty should favor recall.
- Embedding and BM25 retrieval remove similar token amounts but hurt task performance, suggesting semantic similarity alone is not enough for interactive web tasks.
- FocusAgent is evaluated on WorkArena L1 and WebArena using BrowserGym/AgentLab.
- On WorkArena L1, FocusAgent with GPT-4.1 backbone obtains 51.5% SR with 51% pruning compared with 53.0% for full GenericAgent-BT.
- Security setting: DefenseFocusAgent filters malicious observation lines rather than stopping the workflow like a guard-only system.
- On DoomArena Reddit attacks, DefenseFocusAgent sharply reduces attack success rate, especially for popup attacks, but task success can remain low if the popup physically blocks the page.
- Important visual: Figure 1/page 2 shows the two-stage retrieval/action pipeline; Figure 2/page 4 shows line-numbering, CoT line selection, and a pruned AxTree.

---

## Technical contribution

The technical contribution can be summarized as:

```text
page observation problem
→ targeted representation/pruning/training strategy
→ smaller and more useful context for action selection
→ improved grounding or task success
```

This is directly relevant to generalized web automation because the agent must not only plan. It must also select the correct part of a complex webpage and preserve enough evidence to act safely.

---

## Thesis relevance

For **LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction**, this paper supports one of the central S5.2 claims:

```text
The quality of the observation pipeline determines the reliability of the agent.
```

For data extraction specifically, the same observation problem appears when the agent must identify a field, table, label, repeated item, value, or source evidence. If the page representation removes the wrong content or grounds the wrong element, the extraction result becomes unreliable even if the LLM reasoning is strong.

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is:

```text
observation pruning / visual policy / DOM filtering
does not automatically guarantee
structured, source-verifiable, safe web data extraction.
```

The thesis should therefore connect this paper to a larger pipeline where perception, grounding, extraction, verification, and cost control are jointly considered.

---

## Cross-links

| Later section | Connection |
|---|---|
| **S5.2** | Main relevance: observation representation, pruning, grounding, context selection |
| **S5.3** | Better observations improve planning and next-action prediction |
| **S5.4** | Training strategies, synthetic trajectories, RFT, and data mixtures |
| **S5.5** | Failure modes: pruning errors, grounding errors, prompt injection, repeated loops |
| **S6** | Structured web extraction needs grounded fields, source evidence, and page-state preservation |
| **S8** | Cost, latency, reproducibility, open models, safety, and deployability |

---

## Thesis-ready paragraph

FocusAgent contributes to S5.2 by addressing the page-observation bottleneck of web agents. Uses a lightweight LLM retriever to extract task-relevant lines from accessibility-tree observations before the main web agent predicts the next action. The main lesson for the thesis is that web automation depends on the interaction between representation and action: the agent must receive the right page state, in the right size, with enough structure to ground the next operation. FocusAgent reduces observation size by over 50% while maintaining performance close to full-observation baselines, and its defense variant strongly reduces banner/pop-up prompt-injection success rates. However, The retriever is still prompt-sensitive, and aggressive pruning can remove necessary state information; popup attacks can still block task execution even when malicious text is removed. Therefore, this paper should be used to motivate the thesis gap around robust, cost-efficient, and source-verifiable web automation and data extraction.

---

## One-sentence summary

FocusAgent shows that **Uses a lightweight LLM retriever to extract task-relevant lines from accessibility-tree observations before the main web agent predicts the next action**, but the thesis still needs extraction-oriented verification and robust live-web deployment.

---

# Prune4Web: DOM Tree Pruning Programming for Web Agent

## Metadata

- **Short name:** Prune4Web
- **Authors:** Jiayuan Zhang, Kaiquan Chen, Zhihao Lu, Enshen Zhou, Qian Yu, Jing Zhang
- **Year:** 2026
- **Venue/status:** AAAI 2026 Technical Track / Proceedings of the AAAI Conference on Artificial Intelligence, 40(41), 34710–34718
- **DOI:** 10.1609/aaai.v40i41.40772
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2511.21398
- **Venue/status source:** AAAI proceedings page + arXiv
- **S5.2 cluster:** Programmatic DOM pruning for precise grounding
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `prune4web2026`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Transforms DOM pruning from LLM-based reading/ranking into programmatic pruning: an LLM generates lightweight Python scoring programs that filter DOM elements for a low-level sub-task.
```

For the thesis, this paper is important because S5.2 is about what the agent actually receives as its page state before deciding. A web agent does not fail only because it cannot reason; it often fails because the page observation is too long, too noisy, too visual, too dynamic, or not grounded to the correct element.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Transforms DOM pruning from LLM-based reading/ranking into programmatic pruning: an LLM generates lightweight Python scoring programs that filter DOM elements for a low-level sub-task.

- **Key finding:**  
  Prune4Web reduces candidate DOM elements by 25–50× and improves low-level grounding accuracy from 46.80% without pruning to 88.28% with programmatic filtering.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It depends on correct low-level sub-task decomposition and on scoring programs retaining the true target element; it is mainly evaluated around action grounding rather than full extraction verification.

- **Connects to:**  
  Mind2Web, Multimodal-Mind2Web, WebAgent, LCoW, FocusAgent, S5.3 planning, S6 extraction.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as a key S5.2 paper showing that DOM precision can be kept without feeding the full DOM into the LLM.

---

## Detailed notes

- Problem: modern DOMs can be 10,000–100,000 tokens, causing context overflow, latency, and attention dilution.
- Framework stages: Planner → Programmatic Element Filter → Action Grounder.
- The Planner uses high-level task, screenshot, and history to produce low-level sub-tasks without reading the full HTML.
- The Programmatic Element Filter generates parameters for a Python scoring function that ranks DOM candidates.
- The scoring template uses visual text, trusted attributes such as aria-label/name/placeholder, other attributes such as class/id, and match qualities such as exact/phrase/word/fuzzy.
- The Action Grounder receives the low-level sub-task plus the pruned candidate list, then selects the executable action.
- Training uses re-annotated Multimodal-Mind2Web data with low-level sub-tasks, keyword weights, pruned DOMs, and grounding thoughts.
- The two-turn dialogue unified model jointly optimizes planner, filter, and grounder.
- RFT improves planning by using downstream filtering/grounding success as intermediate reward.
- Important visual: Figure 1/page 1 contrasts existing multi-modal web-agent grounding with Prune4Web pruning; Figure 2/page 4 shows the full pipeline from screenshot+DOM to scoring program to final click action.

---

## Technical contribution

The technical contribution can be summarized as:

```text
page observation problem
→ targeted representation/pruning/training strategy
→ smaller and more useful context for action selection
→ improved grounding or task success
```

This is directly relevant to generalized web automation because the agent must not only plan. It must also select the correct part of a complex webpage and preserve enough evidence to act safely.

---

## Thesis relevance

For **LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction**, this paper supports one of the central S5.2 claims:

```text
The quality of the observation pipeline determines the reliability of the agent.
```

For data extraction specifically, the same observation problem appears when the agent must identify a field, table, label, repeated item, value, or source evidence. If the page representation removes the wrong content or grounds the wrong element, the extraction result becomes unreliable even if the LLM reasoning is strong.

---

## Limitation as thesis gap

The remaining gap is:

```text
observation pruning / visual policy / DOM filtering
does not automatically guarantee
structured, source-verifiable, safe web data extraction.
```

The thesis should therefore connect this paper to a larger pipeline where perception, grounding, extraction, verification, and cost control are jointly considered.

Therefore, this paper should be used to motivate the thesis gap around robust, cost-efficient, and source-verifiable web automation and data extraction.

# MolmoWeb: Open Visual Web Agent and Open Data for the Open Web

## Metadata

- **Short name:** MolmoWeb
- **Authors:** Tanmay Gupta, Piper Wolters, Zixian Ma, Peter Sushko, Rock Yuren Pang, Diego Llanes, Yue Yang, Taira Anderson, Boyuan Zheng, Zhongzheng Ren, Harsh Trivedi, Taylor Blanton, Caleb Ouellette, Winson Han, Ali Farhadi, Ranjay Krishna
- **Year:** 2026
- **Venue/status:** arXiv preprint / open technical report; code, models, data, and evaluation harness released on GitHub/Hugging Face
- **DOI:** 10.48550/arXiv.2604.08516
- **arXiv ID:** arXiv:2604.08516
- **Venue/status source:** arXiv + AllenAI GitHub repository
- **S5.2 cluster:** Fully open screenshot-only visual web agent and dataset
- **Priority:** P1
- **BibTeX key:** `molmoweb2026`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper belongs to **S5.2: Perception, Grounding, and Web-State Representation**.

The main idea is:

```text
Introduces MolmoWebMix, a large open mixture of web task trajectories and GUI perception data, and MolmoWeb, 4B/8B screenshot-only visual web agents trained as instruction-conditioned action policies.
```

For the thesis, this paper is important because S5.2 is about what the agent actually receives as its page state before deciding. A web agent does not fail only because it cannot reason; it often fails because the page observation is too long, too noisy, too visual, too dynamic, or not grounded to the correct element.

---

## Four-note template

- **Core idea:**  
  Introduces MolmoWebMix, a large open mixture of web task trajectories and GUI perception data, and MolmoWeb, 4B/8B screenshot-only visual web agents trained as instruction-conditioned action policies.

- **Key finding:**  
  MolmoWeb-8B outperforms similar-scale open-weight web agents and some much larger SoM-based closed-model agents; pass@4 test-time scaling reaches 94.7% on WebVoyager and 60.5% on Online-Mind2Web.

- **Limitation connected to thesis:**  
  It is a recent preprint; while open and strong, the model still shows OCR failures, ambiguous-instruction failures, repeated-action loops, and latency/deployment challenges.

- **Connects to:**  
  Pix2Struct, Pix2Act, WebVoyager, SoM, Holo1, UI-TARS, Fara, S8 reproducibility/deployment, S6 visual extraction.

- **Use in thesis:**  
  Use as the strongest S5.2 P1 open-web agent paper: it connects visual observation, open training data, GUI perception, and reproducible web-agent evaluation.

---

## Detailed notes

- MolmoWeb operates only from screenshots, task instruction, URL/title, and recent action history; it does not require HTML, AxTree, or APIs.
- MolmoWebMix combines synthetic trajectories, human demonstrations, atomic web-skill trajectories, GUI grounding data, and screenshot QA.
- The paper emphasizes openness: model checkpoints, training data, code, and evaluation harness.
- Data sources include AxTree-agent trajectories, multi-agent harness trajectories, human trajectories, node-traversal trajectories, atomic skills, grounding QA, and screenshot QA.
- Atomic skill taxonomy includes go_to, search, find, find_and_open, find_and_click, fill_form, fill_form_and_submit, apply_filters, apply_filters_and_search, add_to_cart, and navigate.
- GUI perception data includes more than 7M grounding QA pairs and over 2.2M screenshot QA pairs.
- The action space includes goto, mouse_click, mouse_drag_and_drop, scroll, scroll_at, hover_at, keyboard_type, keyboard_press, go_back, new_tab, tab_focus, noop, and send_msg_to_user.
- Evaluated on WebVoyager, Online-Mind2Web, DeepShop, and WebTailBench.
- MolmoWeb-8B reports 78.2 on WebVoyager pass@1 and 94.7 pass@4; Online-Mind2Web improves from 35.3 pass@1 to 60.5 pass@4.
- Important visuals: Figure 1/page 2 shows screenshot-only action prediction; Figure 2/page 3 summarizes GUI perception and trajectory data; Figure 6/page 11 shows test-time scaling.

---

```text
page observation problem
→ targeted representation/pruning/training strategy
→ smaller and more useful context for action selection
→ improved grounding or task success
```

This is directly relevant to generalized web automation because the agent must not only plan. It must also select the correct part of a complex webpage and preserve enough evidence to act safely.

# S5.2 P1 — Addendum after Part 2

## What changed after adding Part 2

Part 1 established the main S5.2 landscape:

```text
HTML/markup representation
→ screenshot parsing
→ GUI grounding
→ native action models
→ contextualization and retrieval
```

Part 2 adds a sharper deployment-oriented argument:

```text
A useful web agent must not only understand the page;
it must reduce the page state without losing task-critical evidence.
```

## New papers added

| Paper | New contribution to S5.2 |
|---|---|
| FocusAgent | AxTree line pruning + security against prompt injection |
| Prune4Web | Programmatic DOM pruning via generated scoring programs |
| MolmoWeb | Fully open screenshot-only visual web agent and open data |

## Updated central S5.2 gap

```text
Despite strong progress in visual grounding, contextualization, pruning,
and open visual web agents, current systems still do not jointly solve:
compact observation + reliable grounding + live-web robustness
+ structured extraction correctness + source verification
+ security against injected content + cost efficiency.
```

This updated gap should be reused when refining the full S5.2 P1 synthesis.
