## 4. Main narrative of S5.2 P1

The refined S5.2 P1 narrative is:

```text
The field moves from structured webpage understanding
to multimodal perception,
then to specialized grounding models,
then to compact observation pipelines,
and finally to open visual web agents trained end-to-end.
```

In simpler terms:

```text
HTML/DOM understanding
→ screenshot parsing
→ visual grounding
→ GUI action models
→ observation pruning/contextualization
→ open visual web agents
```

This is the technical evolution after SeeAct.

SeeAct showed the problem:

```text
good plan + bad grounding = failed web agent
```

S5.2 P1 shows the attempted solutions:

```text
better representations
better grounding data
better GUI VLMs
better screen parsers
better context reduction
better open training data
```

The main conclusion is:

```text
Grounding is not a single module.
It is an end-to-end property of the observation, model, action space, and training data.
```

---

## 5. Refined S5.2 taxonomy

| Cluster | Papers | Main idea | Main limitation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Markup/HTML representation | MarkupLM, Understanding HTML with LLMs | Use HTML, XML, XPath, and raw markup as structured signals | HTML is long, noisy, and not always aligned with visual rendering |
| Screenshot parsing | Pix2Struct, Pix2Act | Learn from screenshots and map pixels to structure/actions | Pixel-only methods may miss semantic DOM attributes |
| Visual prompting | Set-of-Mark | Add labels/marks to visual regions to make grounding speakable | Dense webpages can cause label hallucination or wrong label association |
| GUI grounding pretraining | SeeClick, UGround, OS-Atlas, GUI-Actor | Train models to map instructions/referring expressions to GUI targets | Grounding improves but does not guarantee task-level success |
| Screen parsing | OmniParser | Convert screenshots into structured regions for pure-vision agents | Parser errors become downstream action errors |
| Native GUI / VLA models | CogAgent, ShowUI, UI-TARS, Magma | Train integrated vision-language-action models | Expensive, data-hungry, and not extraction-verification focused |
| End-to-end visual web agents | WebVoyager, Surfer-H, MolmoWeb | Operate live websites from screenshots and action history | OCR, latency, repeated loops, and safety remain hard |
| Contextualization / pruning | LCoW, LineRetriever, FocusAgent, Prune4Web | Reduce observation length while preserving task-relevant information | Pruning can remove necessary evidence or fail under distribution shift |

---

## 6. Cluster synthesis

## 6.1 Markup and HTML representation

### MarkupLM

MarkupLM is important because it represents an earlier but still relevant direction: using markup structure as a signal for webpage and document understanding. Instead of treating HTML/XML as plain text, MarkupLM encodes the markup hierarchy through XPath-style embeddings.

Its thesis value is:

```text
HTML structure contains useful semantic and extraction signals.
```

This is directly relevant to web data extraction because extraction often depends on:

```text
labels
tables
forms
repeated item structures
attribute names
DOM hierarchy
```

However, MarkupLM is not a web agent. It is a representation model for visually rich document and webpage understanding. It does not handle live navigation, dynamic states, or action grounding.

### Understanding HTML with Large Language Models

This paper studies whether LLMs can understand raw HTML for element classification, description generation, and web navigation. It shows that pretrained language models can transfer well to HTML understanding and can be sample-efficient on web-navigation tasks.

Its thesis value is:

```text
raw HTML is useful for LLM-based agents,
but raw HTML alone is not enough.
```

The limitation is that real pages contain very long and noisy HTML. Also, HTML may not capture what the user visually sees after rendering, scrolling, overlays, lazy loading, or dynamic scripts.

### Cluster lesson

The markup/HTML cluster establishes one side of S5.2:

```text
DOM/HTML gives precision and structure.
```

But it also exposes the main problem:

```text
DOM/HTML is too long, noisy, and sometimes visually misaligned.
```

This motivates later methods for pruning, contextualization, visual grounding, and screenshot-based agents.

---

## 6.2 Screenshot parsing and pixel-based UI action

### Pix2Struct

Pix2Struct introduces screenshot parsing as a pretraining task. It learns to reconstruct simplified HTML from webpage screenshots. This is important because it turns the web itself into a large source of visual-language supervision.

Its thesis value is:

```text
web screenshots can train models to understand layout, text, and structure.
```

This creates a foundation for screenshot-based GUI agents.

### Pix2Act

Pix2Act moves from screenshot parsing to action prediction. It uses screenshots and generic mouse/keyboard actions instead of DOM or task-specific action spaces.

Its thesis value is:

```text
agents can act from pixels, closer to how humans use GUIs.
```

This matters for deployment because many real systems may not expose clean DOM, accessibility trees, APIs, or stable element identifiers.

However, pixel-only action has limitations:

```text
it can be brittle under visual changes
it may miss semantic attributes
it can struggle with hidden or off-screen information
it does not directly provide structured extraction evidence
```

### Cluster lesson

The screenshot-parsing cluster establishes the opposite side of S5.2:

```text
screenshots give human-like visual perception.
```

But screenshots alone are not enough either:

```text
they are compact but semantically incomplete.
```

This motivates multimodal or hybrid approaches that combine visual perception with DOM/AxTree structure.

---

## 6.3 Visual prompting and Set-of-Mark grounding

### Set-of-Mark Prompting

Set-of-Mark prompting adds explicit labels, masks, boxes, or marks to image regions so that GPT-4V can refer to them in text. This is widely used in multimodal web agents because it converts visual grounding into a more language-friendly selection problem.

Its thesis value is:

```text
visual regions become addressable by language.
```

This is important because an LMM can say:

```text
click mark 17
```

instead of outputting raw coordinates.

But SeeAct and later web-agent work show the limitation:

```text
dense webpages can cause hallucinated marks,
wrong label associations,
and confusion between nearby elements.
```

SoM is useful but not sufficient for reliable web grounding.

### Cluster lesson

Visual prompting is a practical bridge between screenshots and actions, but it remains a prompting technique. It does not fully solve element identity, DOM alignment, or long-horizon reliability.

---

## 6.4 GUI grounding pretraining

This is the core cluster of S5.2 P1.

It includes:

```text
SeeClick
UGround
OS-Atlas
GUI-Actor
```

### SeeClick

SeeClick argues that GUI grounding is the foundation of visual GUI agents. It trains agents to click elements from screenshots and introduces ScreenSpot for realistic GUI grounding evaluation.

Its thesis value is:

```text
better grounding correlates with better downstream GUI-agent performance.
```

This directly supports the SeeAct conclusion. Grounding is not an implementation detail; it is a core capability.

### UGround

UGround trains a universal visual grounding model for GUI agents across web, desktop, and mobile. It maps referring expressions to pixel coordinates.

Its thesis value is:

```text
specialized grounding models can reduce the oracle-grounding gap.
```

UGround is especially important because it explicitly follows from the SeeAct problem: if the main agent can plan but not ground, train a dedicated visual grounder.

### OS-Atlas

OS-Atlas scales GUI grounding and action modeling across platforms. It curates millions of GUI elements and trains a foundation action model for web, desktop, and mobile.

Its thesis value is:

```text
grounding may require large-scale, cross-platform GUI data.
```

This shows that generalist agents need broad interface data, not only natural-language reasoning data.

### GUI-Actor

GUI-Actor argues that coordinate-string generation has limitations. Instead of making the model output coordinates directly as text, it introduces coordinate-free grounding through an attention-based action head over visual tokens.

Its thesis value is:

```text
the action representation itself affects grounding generalization.
```

This is important because many agents fail by producing slightly wrong coordinates, especially under layout changes or high-resolution screens.

### Cluster lesson

The GUI grounding cluster shows the main technical direction after SeeAct:

```text
make grounding a trained capability, not only a prompt trick.
```

However, even strong grounding models do not solve the whole thesis problem. They localize elements, but generalized web data extraction also requires:

```text
schema adherence
field-level correctness
source evidence
verification
cost control
safe execution
```

---

## 6.5 Screen parsing for pure-vision agents

### OmniParser

OmniParser converts UI screenshots into structured interactable regions and semantic labels. It supports pure-vision agents that do not use DOM or accessibility trees.

Its thesis value is:

```text
screen parsing can create a synthetic structure from pixels.
```

This matters when DOM/AxTree is unavailable, unreliable, or too expensive. OmniParser acts as a middle layer:

```text
raw screenshot
→ parsed UI elements
→ grounded action selection
```

The limitation is that parser errors become agent errors. If OmniParser misses a button, mislabels an icon, or creates wrong boxes, the downstream LMM can act incorrectly.

### Cluster lesson

Screen parsers are useful because they recreate structure from vision. But they introduce a new dependency:

```text
the agent is only as reliable as its parser.
```

For the thesis, this supports a broader S5.2 point: every representation layer has failure modes.

---

## 6.6 Native GUI and vision-language-action models

This cluster includes:

```text
CogAgent
ShowUI
UI-TARS
Magma
```

### CogAgent

CogAgent is a high-resolution VLM specialized for GUI understanding and navigation. It shows that GUI-specific visual training improves screen perception and action prediction.

Its thesis value is:

```text
web/GUI agents need interface-specific visual training,
not only generic image-language pretraining.
```

### ShowUI

ShowUI builds a lightweight vision-language-action model with UI-guided visual token selection. It emphasizes efficiency by reducing redundant visual tokens.

Its thesis value is:

```text
visual grounding must also be cost-efficient.
```

This matters because multimodal agents can be expensive in real deployment, especially when screenshots are processed at every step.

### UI-TARS

UI-TARS moves toward native GUI agents trained end-to-end with screenshots, unified action modeling, reasoning, memory, and reflection.

Its thesis value is:

```text
the field is shifting from modular prompting frameworks to native GUI action models.
```

It is important but should be cited carefully because it is a technical report/preprint rather than a final peer-reviewed paper.

### Magma

Magma is broader than web agents. It trains a multimodal foundation model for agentic action across images, videos, UI, and robotics, using Set-of-Mark and Trace-of-Mark signals.

Its thesis value is:

```text
web/GUI automation is part of a larger shift toward multimodal action foundation models.
```

However, its broad scope means it should not be used as the main evidence for web extraction. It is better as a trend paper.

### Cluster lesson

Native GUI/action models reduce the need for hand-built modules, but they do not automatically solve:

```text
structured extraction
verification
source attribution
safe deployment
live-web robustness
```

So they are powerful but not sufficient for the thesis problem.

---

## 6.7 End-to-end multimodal web agents

This cluster includes:

```text
WebVoyager
Surfer-H / Holo1
MolmoWeb
```

### WebVoyager

WebVoyager builds an end-to-end multimodal web agent using screenshots and marked interactive elements. It operates on real websites and completes tasks using actions such as click, type, scroll, and answer.

Its thesis value is:

```text
S5.2 grounding methods can be deployed inside live web-agent systems.
```

It connects S5.2 to S5.3 because perception and grounding are used for actual multi-step decision-making.

However, WebVoyager relies on closed-source LMMs and marked elements. It shows practical ability but not full reproducibility or extraction verification.

### Surfer-H / Holo1

Surfer-H focuses on cost-efficient open-weight web agents. It uses screenshot-only modules such as policy, localizer, validator, and memory.

Its thesis value is:

```text
web-agent perception must become cheaper and deployable,
not only accurate.
```

This connects S5.2 to S8 because cost and latency matter for real deployment.

### MolmoWeb

MolmoWeb is one of the strongest P1 papers for the open-web direction. It introduces MolmoWebMix and MolmoWeb, fully open screenshot-only web agents trained on synthetic trajectories, human demonstrations, atomic skills, grounding data, and screenshot QA.

Its thesis value is:

```text
open data + targeted visual web-agent training can produce competitive screenshot-only agents.
```

MolmoWeb is important because it directly addresses reproducibility. Many powerful web agents are proprietary, but MolmoWeb releases model/data/code/evaluation artifacts.

It also shows the value of:

```text
GUI perception data
task trajectories
atomic web skills
test-time scaling
open evaluation harnesses
```

However, MolmoWeb still has limitations: OCR errors, ambiguous instructions, repeated-action loops, latency, and incomplete recovery.

### Cluster lesson

End-to-end visual web agents show that S5.2 mechanisms matter in deployed browser agents. But even strong visual agents are still vulnerable to:

```text
OCR failure
wrong click localization
repeated loops
cost/latency
weak recovery
uncertain source verification
```

---

## 6.8 Contextualization, retrieval, and observation reduction

This cluster includes:

```text
LCoW
LineRetriever
FocusAgent
Prune4Web
```

This is one of the most thesis-relevant clusters because generalized web automation and data extraction require long-page handling.

### LCoW

LCoW learns to contextualize webpages for LLM agents. Instead of giving the agent the full raw observation, it rewrites the page into a focused, task-relevant observation.

Its thesis value is:

```text
observation simplification can improve decision-making.
```

This is important because the bottleneck is not always the agent’s reasoning. Sometimes the raw page state is too noisy.

### LineRetriever

LineRetriever retrieves planning-relevant observation lines from AxTree/DOM-like representations. It argues that generic embedding retrieval is insufficient because interactive tasks require planning-aware context.

Its thesis value is:

```text
the agent needs task-relevant lines, not merely semantically similar lines.
```

This directly supports the thesis need for cost-efficient extraction over long pages.

### FocusAgent

FocusAgent extends observation reduction and adds a security dimension. It prunes accessibility-tree lines before action prediction and can remove malicious prompt-injection content from the observation.

Its thesis value is:

```text
observation pruning is both a cost mechanism and a security mechanism.
```

This is important for S8 because deployable agents must handle malicious or irrelevant webpage content.

However, FocusAgent also shows that filtering text is not enough when the UI itself is blocked by a popup or overlay. Removing malicious text from the observation does not physically remove the obstruction.

### Prune4Web

Prune4Web keeps DOM precision but avoids feeding massive DOMs to the LLM. It uses an LLM to generate lightweight Python scoring programs that prune DOM elements based on low-level sub-task clues.

Its thesis value is:

```text
programmatic pruning can preserve DOM precision while avoiding attention dilution.
```

This is highly relevant to data extraction because extraction often needs precise DOM element selection.

Prune4Web is especially important because it offers a middle path:

```text
not full DOM reading
not screenshot-only guessing
but task-specific DOM candidate pruning
```

### Cluster lesson

The contextualization/pruning cluster provides a key S5.2 conclusion:

```text
the agent should not read everything;
it should read the right things.
```

But this remains risky. If pruning removes the wrong element or evidence, the agent may fail silently.

For data extraction, this matters directly:

```text
pruning must preserve extraction evidence, not only action candidates.
```

---

## 7. Full refined S5.2 comparison table

| Direction | Representative papers | Observation type | Main strength | Main weakness |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Markup-aware representation | MarkupLM | HTML/XML + XPath | Captures document hierarchy | Not an agent; no live grounding |
| Raw HTML LLM understanding | Understanding HTML with LLMs | Raw HTML | Shows LLMs can learn HTML semantics | Long/noisy context; weak visual grounding |
| Screenshot parsing | Pix2Struct | Screenshot → text/HTML | Learns visual structure from web screenshots | Not an agent by itself |
| Pixel UI action | Pix2Act | Screenshot only | Human-like mouse/keyboard action | Limited real-web generalization |
| Visual marking | Set-of-Mark | Marked screenshot | Makes regions referenceable | Dense UI label confusion |
| GUI grounding | SeeClick, UGround, OS-Atlas, GUI-Actor | Screenshot / GUI targets | Improves target localization | Does not guarantee task success |
| Screen parsing | OmniParser | Screenshot → regions/labels | Adds structure without DOM | Parser errors propagate |
| End-to-end visual web agent | WebVoyager, Surfer-H, MolmoWeb | Screenshot + action history | Practical live web use | OCR, loops, latency, safety |
| Native visual-action model | CogAgent, ShowUI, UI-TARS, Magma | Screenshot/video + action data | Integrated perception/action | Expensive; extraction verification absent |
| Observation contextualization | LCoW, LineRetriever, FocusAgent | AxTree/DOM reduced context | Cost reduction and focused state | May remove needed evidence |
| Programmatic DOM pruning | Prune4Web | DOM + generated code | Precise, interpretable pruning | Depends on sub-task and scoring quality |

---

## 8. Central technical lessons from S5.2 P1

## 8.1 HTML and DOM are precise but too large

DOM and HTML provide important semantic structure, but real pages can contain tens of thousands of tokens. This creates:

```text
context overflow
attention dilution
latency
irrelevant noise
loss of important elements through truncation
```

This motivates pruning and contextualization.

## 8.2 Screenshots are compact but semantically incomplete

Screenshots represent what humans see and avoid DOM token explosion. But they introduce:

```text
OCR errors
coordinate grounding errors
hidden element blindness
difficulty with small text
difficulty with semantic attributes
difficulty with off-screen content
```

This motivates GUI grounding data, screen parsing, and multimodal methods.

## 8.3 Grounding is a learned capability

SeeAct showed the grounding gap. S5.2 P1 papers show that grounding can be improved through:

```text
grounding datasets
referring-expression training
coordinate prediction
attention-based action heads
screen parsing
Set-of-Mark prompting
cross-platform GUI data
```

But grounding still does not equal full task completion.

## 8.4 Observation reduction is now central

LCoW, LineRetriever, FocusAgent, and Prune4Web show that observation reduction is becoming a central agent component.

This changes the web-agent pipeline:

```text
perceive everything
```

becomes:

```text
select task-relevant evidence before reasoning
```

For the thesis, this is critical because extraction agents must handle long pages and repeated structures.

## 8.5 Open data and reproducibility are becoming more important

MolmoWeb is important because it makes the open-science issue explicit. If web-agent performance depends on undisclosed proprietary data and hidden training recipes, the field cannot fully understand what drives progress.

For the thesis, this supports a methodology point:

```text
generalized web automation needs reproducible evaluation and open training data,
especially for academic research.
```

---

## 9. Central S5.2 P1 gap

The central gap after S5.2 P1 is:

```text
No current method jointly solves:
robust visual-DOM grounding
+ compact task-relevant observation
+ long-context web-state management
+ live-web robustness
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ safety against malicious page content
+ low cost and latency
+ reproducible open evaluation
```

Each cluster solves part of the problem:

```text
MarkupLM / HTML LLMs       → structure and markup semantics
Pix2Struct / Pix2Act       → screenshot representation and pixel action
SoM / SeeClick / UGround   → visual grounding
OS-Atlas / GUI-Actor       → scalable/corrected action grounding
OmniParser                 → screen parsing
WebVoyager / Surfer-H      → live multimodal agents
MolmoWeb                   → open screenshot-only web agent and data
LCoW / LineRetriever       → contextualization and line retrieval
FocusAgent                 → pruning and prompt-injection mitigation
Prune4Web                  → programmatic DOM pruning
```

But none fully solves generalized web automation and data extraction.

---

## 10. Explicit extraction-focused gap

The most thesis-specific point is:

```text
S5.2 methods improve perception and grounding,
but they rarely evaluate structured web data extraction as the primary objective.
```

For extraction, the agent must not only click correctly. It must:

```text
identify the correct source region
select the correct DOM or visual evidence
extract the correct value
normalize the value
follow a schema
verify the source
handle repeated items and tables
avoid injected or irrelevant content
measure completeness
track provenance
```

Current S5.2 papers help with components of this pipeline, but they do not provide the full extraction solution.

Therefore, S5.2 should feed directly into S6:

```text
Grounding is necessary for extraction,
but extraction also requires schema, verification, provenance, and completeness.
```

---

S5.1 showed that benchmarks increasingly test:

real websites
visual grounding
live environments
multi-turn dialogue
deep research
safety
domain-specific extraction-like tasks

S5.2 explains why agents still fail on these benchmarks:

```text
wrong representation
wrong target element
too much observation noise
missing visual evidence
missed DOM attributes
pruned-away useful content
coordinate errors
OCR failures
repeated action loops
```

So the connection is:

```text
S5.1 identifies benchmark failures.
S5.2 explains the perception and grounding causes of those failures.
```

S5.3 will discuss planning and decision-making.

S5.2 prepares that discussion because planning depends on observation quality.

A planner cannot succeed if:

```text
the page state is incomplete
the wrong elements are visible to the model
the relevant evidence was pruned
the screenshot cannot be read
the DOM does not match what is visually rendered
```

So S5.3 should build on this statement:

```text
Planning is only as reliable as the grounded state representation given to the planner.
```

S5.2 P1 gives many concrete failure modes:

These should be reused in S5.5 as perception/grounding failure modes.

S6 is about web data extraction.

S5.2 is crucial for S6 because extraction requires grounded evidence.

A web extraction agent must map:

```text
schema field
→ webpage region
→ DOM/visual evidence
→ extracted value
→ verified output
```

S5.2 papers provide tools for:

finding elements
pruning DOMs
reading screenshots
reducing context

locating source evidence

But S6 must add the missing layer:

structured extraction and verification.
```

## 19. Thesis-ready refined synthesis paragraph

The S5.2 P1 literature shows that perception and grounding are central to the reliability of LLM-based web agents. Early work such as MarkupLM and HTML-oriented LLM studies demonstrates that HTML and markup structure provide important semantic signals for webpage understanding and extraction, but raw DOM representations are often too long, noisy, and visually misaligned for direct use. Screenshot-based work such as Pix2Struct and Pix2Act shifts the field toward visual perception and pixel-level actions, while Set-of-Mark prompting makes visual regions easier for LMMs to reference. Subsequent GUI grounding models such as SeeClick, UGround, OS-Atlas, and GUI-Actor show that grounding must be trained directly as a core capability rather than treated as a minor prompting detail. Screen parsing methods such as OmniParser create structured visual regions without relying on DOM, while native vision-language-action models such as CogAgent, ShowUI, UI-TARS, and Magma integrate perception, reasoning, and action more tightly. At the system level, WebVoyager, Surfer-H, and MolmoWeb demonstrate that multimodal web agents can operate on live websites from screenshots and action history, with MolmoWeb showing the importance of open training data and reproducible evaluation. Finally, LCoW, LineRetriever, FocusAgent, and Prune4Web address the observation bottleneck by contextualizing or pruning long page representations, either through learned summaries, line retrieval, security-aware AxTree filtering, or programmatic DOM scoring. Together, these works show that robust web automation depends on compact, grounded, task-relevant webpage representations. However, they still do not fully solve structured, source-verifiable web data extraction under live-web, safety, and cost constraints.

---

## 20. Final S5.2 P1 synthesis

S5.2 P1 establishes that the webpage observation is one of the main bottlenecks in LLM-based web automation. The agent must decide what to do, but before it can decide, it must perceive the interface in a form that is both compact and faithful. HTML and DOM provide structure but are often too long and noisy. Screenshots provide a human-like view but require OCR, coordinate grounding, and visual reasoning. Accessibility trees are useful but can still be large and vulnerable to irrelevant or malicious content. Screen parsers and Set-of-Mark prompting make visual regions addressable, but they can introduce label or parsing errors. Grounding models improve localization, but they do not guarantee task completion or extraction correctness. Observation-reduction systems reduce cost and noise, but they risk removing task-critical evidence.

The main refined conclusion is:

```text
web-agent grounding is not a single step;
it is the result of the full observation pipeline:
representation, pruning, visual perception, grounding, action encoding, and training data.
```

For the thesis, this means generalized web automation and data extraction require more than stronger LLM reasoning. They require a web-state representation that aligns:

```text
user intent
+ screenshot evidence
+ DOM/HTML structure
+ accessibility information
+ action history
+ extraction schema
+ source verification
```

The remaining gap is therefore clear. Current S5.2 methods improve perception, grounding, and context reduction, but they do not yet provide a complete extraction-oriented agent architecture that is robust across live websites, safe against malicious content, cost-efficient, reproducible, and able to verify structured outputs against source evidence. This gap motivates the transition from S5.2 to S5.3, S5.5, S6, and S8.

# S5.2 P1 Part 1 Mini-Synthesis — Perception, Grounding, and Observation Reduction

## 1. What this batch adds after SeeAct

The S5.2 P0 paper, **SeeAct**, established the core bottleneck:

```text
LMMs can often decide what to do,
but they struggle to ground the action to the exact webpage element.
```

The S5.2 P1 Part 1 papers explain how the field tries to solve that bottleneck.

They expand the problem in five directions:

```text
1. HTML/markup representation
2. Screenshot parsing and visual webpage understanding
3. GUI grounding and coordinate prediction
4. Screen parsing and visual action models
5. Contextualization and observation reduction
```

Together, they show that S5.2 is not just about using screenshots. It is about building the right observation pipeline for agents.

---

## 2. Main technical evolution

### 2.1 From markup-aware models to HTML-aware LLMs

**MarkupLM** and **Understanding HTML with Large Language Models** show that HTML and markup structures contain important semantic signals.

The evolution is:

```text
markup hierarchy / XPath
→ raw HTML understanding
→ semantic classification and element description
→ action prediction over HTML pages
```

This is important for web data extraction because HTML structure can identify fields, tables, labels, forms, and repeated records.

But HTML-only methods remain limited:

```text
HTML is long
HTML is noisy
HTML may be incomplete
HTML may not match the rendered visual page
```

So the field moves toward visual page understanding.

---

### 2.2 From screenshots to structured visual understanding

**Pix2Struct** and **Pix2Act** show that screenshots can be treated as a primary representation.

Pix2Struct learns to parse screenshots into simplified HTML-like structures. Pix2Act then uses this visual pretraining to act in GUIs using only pixels and generic mouse/keyboard actions.

This shift matters because it changes the agent interface:

```text
DOM/action abstractions
→ pixel observations and human-like actions
```

For the thesis, this supports the idea that generalized web automation should not depend only on internal website code. A deployable agent may need to act visually, especially when DOM, APIs, or accessibility trees are unavailable or unreliable.

---

### 2.3 From visual prompting to GUI grounding

**Set-of-Mark**, **SeeClick**, **UGround**, **OS-Atlas**, and **GUI-Actor** all focus on grounding.

The key S5.2 idea is:

```text
the agent's plan must be mapped to a precise target region
```

Different methods solve this differently:

```text
SoM        → overlay labels on visual regions
SeeClick   → train GUI grounding directly
UGround    → train a universal cross-platform grounding model
OS-Atlas   → scale action/grounding data across platforms
GUI-Actor  → avoid coordinate-string generation via attention-based action grounding
```

This cluster confirms the SeeAct result: grounding is not a minor implementation detail. It is a central capability.

---

### 2.4 From modular perception to native visual agents

**CogAgent**, **ShowUI**, **UI-TARS**, and **Magma** move from grounding modules toward native visual/action models.

The trend is:

```text
separate perception + planner + grounding module
→ integrated vision-language-action model
```

These papers add new ingredients:

```text
high-resolution GUI perception
UI-guided visual token selection
unified action spaces
System-2 reasoning traces
reflective online training
multimodal action pretraining
```

For the thesis, these papers are important because they show the future direction of web agents: fewer hand-built wrappers and more data-driven agent models.

But the thesis must be careful: stronger native agents still do not automatically solve structured extraction, source verification, safety, or cost.

---

### 2.5 From raw observations to focused context

**LCoW** and **LineRetriever** address another S5.2 bottleneck: observations are too long and noisy.

Their shared idea is:

```text
do not feed the full page;
feed the parts that matter for the next decision.
```

LCoW contextualizes raw observations into a focused, explanatory representation. LineRetriever retrieves observation lines that are useful for future planning.

This is important for the thesis because generalized web extraction often involves long pages, large DOM trees, repeated elements, and multi-step workflows. Observation reduction is not only a cost trick; it is a reliability mechanism.

---

## 3. Central S5.2 P1 Part 1 gap

This batch shows strong progress, but the central gap remains:

```text
No current method jointly provides:
accurate GUI/web grounding
+ visual-DOM alignment
+ planning-aware context reduction
+ live-web robustness
+ structured extraction correctness
+ source-grounded verification
+ low cost and latency
```

Each group solves one part:

```text
MarkupLM / HTML LLMs → structure
Pix2Struct / Pix2Act → visual representation and pixel actions
SoM / SeeClick / UGround / OS-Atlas / GUI-Actor → grounding
OmniParser → screen parsing
WebVoyager / Surfer-H → live multimodal web agents
LCoW / LineRetriever → observation reduction
UI-TARS / Magma / ShowUI → native visual-action agents
```

But no group fully solves generalized web automation and data extraction.

---

## 4. Thesis-ready synthesis paragraph

The S5.2 P1 literature extends SeeAct's grounding bottleneck into a broader architecture problem. Early representation work such as MarkupLM and HTML-oriented LLMs shows that markup structure and raw HTML contain useful semantic signals for web understanding, but HTML remains noisy, long, and incomplete. Pix2Struct and Pix2Act shift the field toward screenshot-based perception and pixel-level actions, showing that agents can learn from rendered interfaces rather than relying only on DOM trees. Set-of-Mark prompting, SeeClick, UGround, OS-Atlas, and GUI-Actor then identify visual grounding as the central operational capability: the agent must map a textual plan or instruction to the correct screen region. OmniParser adds a complementary screen-parsing layer that converts screenshots into structured regions. WebVoyager and Surfer-H show how these ideas can be deployed in live web agents, while LCoW and LineRetriever address the cost and noise of long web observations through contextualization and planning-aware reduction. Finally, ShowUI, UI-TARS, and Magma indicate a broader shift toward native vision-language-action models that integrate perception, grounding, reasoning, and action. Overall, S5.2 P1 shows that reliable web automation depends on the quality of the agent's observation pipeline, not only on the reasoning ability of the underlying LLM. The remaining thesis gap is to connect robust grounding and observation reduction with structured, source-verifiable web data extraction under live-web constraints.

---

## 5. Cross-links to later sections

| Cluster | Papers | Feeds |
|---|---|---|
| Markup/HTML representation | MarkupLM, Understanding HTML with LLMs | S6 extraction; DOM/schema understanding |
| Screenshot parsing | Pix2Struct, Pix2Act | S5.2 perception; S5.5 visual failures |
| Visual prompting/screen parsing | SoM, OmniParser | S5.2 grounding; S5.5 hallucinated labels |
| GUI grounding | SeeClick, UGround, OS-Atlas, GUI-Actor | S5.2 grounding; S8 deployment |
| End-to-end visual web agents | WebVoyager, Surfer-H | S5.3 planning; S8 cost/live deployment |
| Native action models | CogAgent, ShowUI, UI-TARS, Magma | S5.4 training; S5.3 reasoning |
| Context reduction | LCoW, LineRetriever | S8 cost; S6 extraction context selection |

### D:\PhD\myArticlesAndPapers\Literature-review4\markdowns\S5\S5.2\writing\S5_2_P1_part2_mini_synthesis.md

# S5.2 P1 Part 2 Mini-Synthesis — Observation Bottleneck, Pruning, and Open Visual Web Agents

## 1. Why this part matters

Part 2 is smaller than Part 1, but it is very important for the S5.2 argument.

The three papers focus on the same core problem:

```text
web pages contain too much information for agents to process reliably and cheaply
```

They propose three different solutions:

```text
FocusAgent  → keep only relevant AxTree lines
Prune4Web   → generate programs to prune DOM elements
MolmoWeb    → train a screenshot-only visual web agent and avoid DOM/AxTree dependency
```

This makes Part 2 a strong continuation of the S5.2 P1 theme: agent reliability depends on the observation pipeline.

---

## 2. FocusAgent: pruning as efficiency and security

FocusAgent extends LineRetriever-style observation reduction.

Its key idea is:

```text
use a lightweight LLM retriever
to select the relevant lines of the accessibility tree
before the main agent predicts actions
```

This is useful for two reasons.

First, it reduces cost and context length. Full AxTrees may contain tens of thousands of tokens, while only a fraction is relevant to the current task step.

Second, it can reduce prompt-injection risk. If malicious text is present in banners, popups, SVG alt text, or hidden observation fields, a retrieval stage can remove irrelevant attack content before it reaches the main agent.

The important thesis lesson is:

```text
observation pruning is not only compression;
it is also a safety and robustness mechanism.
```

However, FocusAgent also shows a limitation. If the attack is physically blocking the UI, removing the malicious text from the observation does not necessarily unblock the page. This means that safety-aware observation filtering must be combined with UI-state management.

---

## 3. Prune4Web: DOM pruning as executable program generation

Prune4Web keeps the semantic precision of DOM/HTML but avoids feeding the entire DOM to the LLM.

Its key idea is:

```text
the LLM does not read the whole DOM;
it generates a lightweight Python scoring program
that ranks DOM elements for the current low-level sub-task.
```

The pipeline is:

```text
high-level task + screenshot + history
→ Planner creates low-level sub-task
→ Programmatic Element Filter generates scoring program
→ scoring program ranks DOM elements
→ Action Grounder selects from a small candidate list
```

This is important because it gives a middle path between two weak extremes:

```text
full DOM input       → precise but too long
screenshot-only input → compact but can miss semantic DOM clues
```

Prune4Web is especially relevant to web data extraction because extraction often needs DOM-level precision: labels, form fields, table cells, repeated records, and hidden semantic attributes.

The thesis lesson is:

```text
programmatic pruning can preserve DOM precision while avoiding attention dilution.
```

---

## 4. MolmoWeb: open screenshot-only web agents

MolmoWeb takes a different path: instead of pruning DOM/AxTree, it trains a visual web agent that operates only from screenshots.

Its key idea is:

```text
train a fully open visual-language action policy
using web task trajectories + GUI perception data
```

MolmoWebMix combines:

```text
synthetic trajectories
human demonstrations
atomic web skills
GUI grounding data
screenshot QA
```

This makes MolmoWeb very important for the thesis because it addresses a major weakness of the field: many strong web agents are closed, proprietary, or difficult to reproduce.

MolmoWeb shows that open models and open data can compete strongly with closed web agents, especially when trained on targeted web-agent data.

The thesis lesson is:

```text
open data and task-specific visual training can compensate partly for model scale and proprietary infrastructure.
```

But MolmoWeb still has limitations: OCR errors, ambiguous instruction failures, repeated actions, latency, and incomplete recovery from bad states.

---

## 5. Comparison table

| Paper | Observation type | Main mechanism | Strength | Main limitation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FocusAgent | AxTree | LLM line retrieval | Cost reduction + prompt-injection mitigation | Prompt-sensitive; popups can still block UI |
| Prune4Web | DOM + screenshot | Generated Python scoring programs | Precise element grounding with 25–50× pruning | Depends on low-level sub-task quality |
| MolmoWeb | Screenshot only | Open VLM action policy trained on MolmoWebMix | Fully open, strong visual web agent | OCR/recovery/latency limitations |

---

## 6. Central S5.2 Part 2 gap

These papers improve the observation bottleneck, but they do not fully solve the thesis problem.

The remaining gap is:

```text
current systems optimize observation/action success,
but not yet extraction correctness with source-grounded verification.
```

For the thesis, this is crucial. Generalized web automation is not only “click the right place.” Web data extraction also requires:

```text
identify the correct source region
extract the correct value
preserve evidence
validate against page state
handle dynamic changes
avoid injected or irrelevant content
control cost
```

---

## 7. Thesis-ready synthesis paragraph

The S5.2 P1 Part 2 papers sharpen the observation bottleneck introduced in the earlier S5.2 literature. FocusAgent shows that accessibility-tree observations can be pruned with a lightweight LLM retriever, reducing context size and mitigating prompt-injection risk while maintaining task performance. Prune4Web proposes a more programmatic alternative for DOM-heavy pages: rather than asking an LLM to read or rank a full DOM, it generates executable scoring programs that filter candidate elements for the current low-level sub-task, preserving DOM precision while reducing attention dilution. MolmoWeb takes the opposite route by training fully open screenshot-only web agents on a large mixture of synthetic trajectories, human demonstrations, atomic skills, grounding data, and screenshot QA, showing that open visual web agents can be competitive without HTML, AxTree, or proprietary APIs. Together, these papers show that S5.2 is a central technical layer for web agents: before an agent can plan, act, or extract, it must receive a page representation that is compact, grounded, secure, and task-relevant. The remaining thesis gap is to connect these observation and grounding advances to structured, source-verifiable web data extraction under live-web, safety, and cost constraints.

---

| Paper | Feeds |

| FocusAgent | S5.5 prompt-injection failures; S8 safety and cost; S6 extraction context filtering |
| Prune4Web | S6 DOM-based extraction; S5.3 task decomposition; S5.4 RFT/training; S8 latency |
| MolmoWeb | S5.4 open training data; S5.3 visual action policy; S8 reproducibility/open deployment |
